Checks <name>.vyre.run, or reads which space's name this server serves. A server holds no name: a space is named in the app, and the app tells the server to serve it (names.serve).

- Input:
  - `action` string
  - `name` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `onboard`, `tailnet`

### `onboard.pair`

The pair step: whether this server is paired to you (or you are signed in on this computer) and whether its link is up, direct or through the relay. Read only: pairing itself is done from your Vyre app (wink.pair.server, wink.server.pairing).

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `onboard`, `tailnet`

### `onboard.passkey`

Not available: a server is paired to your Vyre app first. Always answers that.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `onboard`, `tailnet`

### `onboard.setup`

The setup step list the box holds: the ten steps in order, each done, current, skipped or todo, with the current step and whether setup is finished. skip puts one of ai, phone, computers or history aside to finish later (it stays listed as skipped), unskip takes it back, pass says the person has been through history. It never completes a step the box can see for itself.

- Input:
  - `pass` "history"
  - `skip` one of "ai", "phone", "computers", "history"
  - `unskip` one of "ai", "phone", "computers", "history"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `onboard`, `tailnet`

### `onboard.skip`

Skip a step for now; it can be finished later from Settings.

- Input:
  - `step` one of "you", "claude", "pair", "name", "history", "devices", required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `onboard`, `tailnet`

### `onboard.status`

Where the onboarding stands: every step's state and what it needs.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `onboard.you`

Step 1: your name as you like it shown, and your assistant's name. A name that is also a valid vyre.run name becomes the default candidate.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `assistant` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `onboard`, `tailnet`

## planner

### `planner.add`

Add an alarm, timer, reminder, todo, note or event. Times: at (ISO or ms), in_ms for a timer, or wall "HH:MM" with date "YYYY-MM-DD" and repeat {every: day|weekday|week|month|year, days?, interval?, until?}. Or give text ("alarm 7am") to read it, with kind as a hint. Anyone may add alarms, timers, reminders, todos and notes.

- Input:
  - `as` object
  - `assignee` string: give a to-do to this assistant or agent (the person only); it finishes it as its own
  - `at` any: an ISO time (with an offset, or read in the item's zone without one), YYYY-MM-DD, or ms since 1970
  - `body` string
  - `date` string
  - `due` any: an ISO time (with an offset, or read in the item's zone without one), YYYY-MM-DD, or ms since 1970
  - `floating` boolean
  - `in_ms` number
  - `kind` one of "alarm", "timer", "reminder", "todo", "note", "event", "task"
  - `list` string
  - `parent` string
  - `paused` boolean
  - `pinned` boolean
  - `priority` integer
  - `project` string
  - `repeat` object
    - `days` list of integer
    - `every` one of "day", "weekday", "week", "month", "year"
    - `interval` integer
    - `until` string
  - `rrule` string or null
  - `tags` list of string
  - `text` string
  - `thread` string
  - `title` string
  - `tz` string
  - `url` string or null
  - `waits_on` string
  - `wall` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `planner.agenda`

What is on between from and to (today in the planner's zone by default): alarms, reminders, timers and events, the connected calendars' events, and the todos due. Each entry has source ("planner" or the Google account's name), start, end, all_day, where, url. Also returns last_event, the event cursor it is current to. busy: true returns only the busy intervals, merged. next: n returns the next n entries from now.

- Input:
  - `as` object
  - `busy` boolean
  - `from` any: an ISO time (with an offset, or read in the item's zone without one), YYYY-MM-DD, or ms since 1970
  - `next` integer
  - `to` any: an ISO time (with an offset, or read in the item's zone without one), YYYY-MM-DD, or ms since 1970
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `planner.bin`

The events you deleted that can still be restored (planner.delete with restore: true and the id), newest first.

- Input:
  - `as` object
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `planner.calendar.create`

Make an event. Without account it is the planner's own event. With account it is written to that Google calendar through google.calendar.create; attendees mean invites, which wait at the Gate for the user (returns { held, message }). Agents may only ask for an invite (account and attendees).

- Input:
  - `start` any, required: an ISO time (with an offset, or read in the item's zone without one), YYYY-MM-DD, or ms since 1970
  - `title` string, required
  - `account` string
  - `as` object
  - `attendees` string or list of string
  - `end` any: an ISO time (with an offset, or read in the item's zone without one), YYYY-MM-DD, or ms since 1970
  - `project` string
  - `rrule` string
  - `thread` string
  - `tz` string
  - `url` string
  - `where` string
  - `why` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `planner.calendar.sync`

Read the connected Google calendars (a day back to 14 days ahead) into the planner's copy now. It also runs every 15 minutes while an account is connected.

- Input:
  - `as` object
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `planner.delete`

Delete an item. It can be restored (restore: true) for 30 days. An event goes to the records' bin and can be restored from there (restore: true with its id).

- Input:
  - `item` string, required
  - `as` object
  - `restore` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `planner.dismiss`

Stop a firing without finishing a todo. A one-off alarm, timer or reminder ends.

- Input:
  - `as` object
  - `firing` string
  - `item` string
  - `key` string: planner-<item>-<due in seconds>, as planner.upcoming and the push give it
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `planner.done`

Done: acknowledge a firing (an alarm stops ringing; a repeating one keeps its schedule) or finish a todo or reminder. Give firing, item, or the ring's key (a device answering a ring it rang itself).

- Input:
  - `as` object
  - `firing` string
  - `item` string
  - `key` string: planner-<item>-<due in seconds>, as planner.upcoming and the push give it
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `planner.get`

One item and its latest firings, by item or firing id.

- Input:
  - `as` object
  - `firing` string
  - `item` string
  - `key` string: planner-<item>-<due in seconds>, as planner.upcoming and the push give it
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `planner.list`

Items, newest time first: filter by kind, state (open by default; all), list, project, pinned, tag; limit up to 500. cursor: true returns { items, last_event }, the event cursor the list is current to.

- Input:
  - `as` object
  - `cursor` boolean
  - `kind` one of "alarm", "timer", "reminder", "todo", "note", "event", "task"
  - `limit` integer
  - `list` string
  - `pinned` boolean
  - `project` string
  - `state` one of "open", "done", "cancelled", "all"
  - `tag` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `planner.parse`

Read words like "alarm 7am", "timer 10 min" or "remind me to call the printer at 6" into a proposed item { kind, title, at (ms), tz, duration?, repeat? }, { ambiguous, reason } when they cannot be placed, or null. kind is a hint. Answers where it is asked, never forwarded.

- Input:
  - `text` string, required
  - `as` object
  - `kind` one of "alarm", "timer", "reminder", "todo", "note", "event", "task"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `planner.ringing`

What is ringing now, shaped like planner.fired, so a surface that connects late can show its banners. cursor: true returns { ringing, last_event }.

- Input:
  - `as` object
  - `cursor` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `planner.settings`

The planner's zone (floating alarms follow it), escalate_after (minutes, from 1), escalate_max (rings after the first) and event_lead (minutes). With no input, the current settings.

- Input:
  - `as` object
  - `escalate_after` integer
  - `escalate_max` integer
  - `event_lead` integer
  - `follow_device` boolean
  - `timezone` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `planner.snooze`

Snooze a firing or an item: it rings again after minutes (9 by default).

- Input:
  - `as` object
  - `firing` string
  - `item` string
  - `key` string: planner-<item>-<due in seconds>, as planner.upcoming and the push give it
  - `minutes` number
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `planner.upcoming`

Every ring expected in the next hours (48 by default, 1 to 72), for a device to schedule as its own notifications so alarms ring with the box out of reach. Entries: key (planner-<item>-<due>, the push's tag too), item, kind, title, due (seconds), at (ms), loud, snoozed?, start and account for events. Refresh on any planner event but planner.fired, and on foreground. Also returns last_event.

- Input:
  - `as` object
  - `hours` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `planner.update`

Change an item: title, body, list, priority, pinned, tags, project, thread, parent, state (open, done, cancelled) or its time. An agent may change only the items it added.

- Input:
  - `item` string, required
  - `as` object
  - `assignee` string: give a to-do to this assistant or agent (the person only); it finishes it as its own
  - `at` any: an ISO time (with an offset, or read in the item's zone without one), YYYY-MM-DD, or ms since 1970
  - `body` string
  - `date` string
  - `due` any: an ISO time (with an offset, or read in the item's zone without one), YYYY-MM-DD, or ms since 1970
  - `floating` boolean
  - `in_ms` number
  - `kind` string
  - `list` string
  - `parent` string
  - `paused` boolean
  - `pinned` boolean
  - `priority` integer
  - `project` string
  - `repeat` object
    - `days` list of integer
    - `every` one of "day", "weekday", "week", "month", "year"
    - `interval` integer
    - `until` string
  - `rrule` string or null
  - `state` one of "open", "done", "cancelled"
  - `tags` list of string
  - `thread` string
  - `title` string
  - `tz` string
  - `url` string or null
  - `waits_on` string
  - `wall` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

## pluginagent

### `pluginagent.allows`

vyred asks, for a call or a tool listing by the plugin agent: which of these tools does the grant name? Answers { allowed: [tool] } for the ones it does, the rest are refused with not_in_grant.

- Input:
  - `tools` list of string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `pluginagent.ask`

Claude Code on this computer asks, once, to read the person's memory and the sessions of their projects. Files a request for the person to approve (the computer is named by the daemon, one pending ask at a time); changes nothing else. Answers { state: 'granted' | 'waiting' | 'declined' | 'quiet' }.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`

### `pluginagent.decline`

The person says no to Claude Code's request (the card's Don't allow): the waiting ask ends and Claude Code does not ask again until the person turns it back on (pluginagent.on).

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`

### `pluginagent.grant`

The person lets Claude Code on a computer read their memory and every project's sessions, once. Registers the agent, adds it to the Space (a kernel act that needs the person's presence) and writes its key where only this OS user can read it. It never becomes the person.

- Input:
  - `id` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `pluginagent.on`

The person turns Claude Code's request back on after a decline or a revoke (from Access): the next time it runs here it asks again. Grants nothing by itself.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`

### `pluginagent.pending`

What is waiting for the person: [{ id, computer, asked_at, sentence }].

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`

### `pluginagent.revoke`

The person takes Claude Code's reach on this computer away: the key stops working at once, the agent is removed, and it does not ask again until the person turns it back on (pluginagent.on).

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `pluginagent.status`

Whether Claude Code on this computer has been granted its reach, and the plain sentence to say when it has not.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`

### `pluginagent.vouch`

vyred asks: is this the key of the plugin agent it names? Answers { ok, agent }. Never the key.

- Input:
  - `agent` string, required
  - `key` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

## presence

### `presence.capsule.pin`

Pins the Capsule build `vyre capsule install` just signed, so vyred can tell that real build apart from anything else with its own ambiguous, tty-less process shape (its own proof, not ancestry: core/daemon/peer.js's verifiedCapsule). Signed by the Capsule's own enrolled presence key (method "capsule"), the same identity a paired Capsule already proves with, not a new one -- so only the real Capsule, not a model's shell with a same-uid file write, can ever set this.

- Input:
  - `cdhash` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `presence.code`

A one-time code, valid 10 minutes, that enrolls one passkey from the Deck. Needs presence.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `presence.covered`

Whether the device a call came from (its tailnet peer; none for this machine) has a live presence session, since when and until when (ms). The Gate and the Switchboard put it on held items and asks.

- Input:
  - `peer` object
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `presence.enroll`

Enroll a Capsule key (P-256 in the Secure Enclave, alg -7), a device key (P-256 with alg -7, or RSA of 2048 bits or more with alg -257, as Windows Hello makes) or a passkey, by its public key as base64url SPKI DER, a JWK or a Windows BCRYPT RSA blob. Needs presence.

- Input:
  - `kind` one of "capsule", "passkey", "device", required
  - `public_key` string, required
  - `alg` integer
  - `core_proof` string
  - `credential_id` string
  - `device` string
  - `name` string
  - `rp_id` string
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `module`, `space`, `tailnet`
- Needs a person present.

### `presence.grant.mint`

The one-time, five-minute grant that lets one browser enroll the first owner passkey. Only the relay module's checked claim makes it.

- Input:
  - `host` string, required
  - `peer` object or null
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `presence.keys`

The Capsule keys, device keys and passkeys enrolled for proving presence: id, kind, name, when enrolled and last used. Never the keys themselves.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `presence.person.end-paired`

End every paired session and grant of one device, or of all devices when none is named. Only the wink module asks.

- Input:
  - `device` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `presence.person.locked`

The paired devices that are locked after wrong sign-in answers, each with the time the lock ends by itself (ms): { locked: [{ device, until }] }. For the owner's Devices list; a removed device is never listed.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mobile`

### `presence.person.pair-challenge`

A device its owner paired asks for the challenge of its grant, to sign for presence.person.start-paired. A device with no grant gets a random one, so nothing says whether a grant exists.

- Input: none
- Callers: `device`, `relay`, `tailnet`

### `presence.person.pair-grant`

After the owner confirmed a pairing with a presence proof, the one-use grant that lets that device open its person session. Only the wink module may ask, and only for a device its own record says the owner confirmed.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `presence.person.renew-allow`

Lift the lock on a paired device that answered its sign-in challenge wrongly three times, from the owner's own device. The device then renews itself with its key.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `presence.person.revoke`

Sign one browser or app out now, by session id.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `presence.person.rotate`

A paired device's session gets a new secret, signed by the device's key. The old one stops working.

- Input:
  - `n` string, required
  - `sig` string, required
  - `t` string, required
- Callers: `device`, `relay`, `tailnet`

### `presence.person.sessions`

The browsers and apps signed in as the person: id, how (cookie or app), device, made, last used, when it lapses. Never a secret.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `presence.person.start`

Sign this browser or app in as the person for 30 days (90 at most), on this device only, with a passkey or the device's own key. The Deck gets a cookie; with cc (a PKCE S256 challenge) the answer is a one-time code the app trades at /v1/person/token; a device paired over the relay sends its request-signing key (key, an ES256 public JWK) and gets the token itself.

- Input:
  - `cc` string
  - `key` object
  - `label` string
  - `return` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `deck`
- Needs a person present.

### `presence.person.start-paired`

A device its owner paired opens its person session: it signs `paired-start`, its id and the challenge of its grant with the key the owner confirmed. No prompt. Answers the token, or one refusal whatever the reason.

- Input:
  - `sig` string, required
  - `esig` string
  - `label` string
- Callers: `device`, `relay`, `tailnet`

### `presence.person.status`

Whether this request is signed in as the person (a person session), and until when.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `presence.person.strength`

The strength of a live person session, for a module that relays a paired device's act to a person-only tool: { strength: one of STRENGTHS | null }. Only pluginagent asks.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `presence.remove`

Remove an enrolled Capsule key, device key or passkey by id. Needs presence.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `module`, `space`, `tailnet`
- Needs a person present.

### `presence.session.close`

End a presence session now.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `presence.session.open`

After one strong proof (Touch ID, the Capsule, a device key or a passkey), a secret that proves presence for revealing, copying, TOTP codes and sends at the Gate for 30 minutes, on this device only.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

## projects

### `projects.access.check`

Whether a named agent may reach a project's data: deny by default, asked of the kernel (a grant on the project's record or on every project). Returns { granted }.

- Input:
  - `agent` string, required
  - `project` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `projects.access.grant`

Let an agent reach a project's data (Drive, synced sessions, anything project-scoped asks projects.access.check before serving an agent): a kernel grant of project.reach on the project's record, a person's own act with the kernel's proof. agent left out or empty grants every agent that exists now.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
  - `agent` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `projects.access.list`

Every project reach grant on record, for a project or every project, for the owner to review who can reach what.

- Input:
  - `project` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `projects.access.pending`

What agents could reach before reach became a kernel grant, still waiting for your approval to carry over: { rows, grants }. Read only.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `projects.access.restore`

Carry what your agents could reach before into kernel grants, in your own call: every granted row becomes a project.reach grant (an agent by its stable id), an explicit revoke stays revoked, and the old rows are then cleared. { restored, skipped }.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `projects.access.revoke`

Take an agent's (or, agent left out, every agent's) access to a project away: its kernel grant is revoked, which also takes away anything delegated from it. An agent that held ONE grant on every project keeps every OTHER project as explicit grants. Instant for the person.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
  - `agent` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `projects.add-threads`

Pick threads (Claude Code session ids) into a project. A thread can be in several projects.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
  - `threads` list of string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `projects.add-workspace`

Attach an existing folder to an existing project as one of its workspaces (Vyre Drive step 4): the folder starts counting as the project's own, the same as one listed at projects.create time. For confirming sync.consent's proposed folder-to-project mapping, or attaching any other folder by hand. Person-only, instant, no presence: a placement decision, same weight as a pick. Refuses a project that does not exist; a folder that resolves to the project's own home is a no-op (added: null), not an error. callers is the person's own surfaces plus sync's and github's own doors (module:sync, module:github), the same named exception as projects.create; every other module is refused.

- Input:
  - `folder` string, required
  - `project` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `projects.adopt`

This computer learns of a Project record: a local row and a home folder for it. Only the work module calls it.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `slug` string, required
- Callers: `module`

### `projects.archive`

Archive a project: it leaves the project list, and its folder, threads, teammates and history are untouched. archived: false brings it back. projects.list {archived: true} includes archived projects. Person-only: a model (agent or session) is refused.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
  - `archived` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `projects.backup.sources`

What the Basic encrypted backup reads of this computer's projects: { items: [{ kind: "file", name, size, mtime, path } and the project rows as rows/projects.jsonl], notices }. Ignore rules applied (build output, dependency folders, caches, logs), files over 2 GB skipped with a notice. The memory module's own.

- Input: none
- Callers: `module`

### `projects.catalog`

Every session on this device for picking into projects, with its /rename name, first message, folder, last activity, projects, and live (a terminal has it open now). q searches names, first messages, folders and, through Recall, what was said.

- Input:
  - `human` boolean
  - `limit` integer
  - `machines` "all" or "local"
  - `q` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `projects.context`

The brief for a thread starting in a project, as plain text for Claude: what the project is, its people, its other threads and its memory. Give project, or cwd and session as a SessionStart hook sees them.

- Input:
  - `cwd` string
  - `project` string
  - `session` string
- Callers: any caller

### `projects.create`

Make a project by hand: a name, a home folder (default: a new folder in the projects folder), other folders it owns, the threads picked into it, and its people. from_thread: the chat this project is made from; it is picked in and its id becomes the project's avatar_seed, so the chat's tile carries over (otherwise the seed is the new slug). Every projects: "*" agent (never the assistant, whose "*" is a different rule) is granted projects.access on it at once too, option (a) (the lead's decision, so agents.projects and projects.access never drift apart): a wildcard agent reads a brand-new project the moment it exists, with no separate step. callers is the person's own surfaces plus sync's and github's own doors (module:sync, module:github), for their own proposed folder-to-project mappings; every other module is refused.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `from_thread` string
  - `home` string
  - `org` string
  - `people` list of object
    - `email` string
    - `name` string
  - `threads` list of string
  - `watchers` list of string
  - `workspaces` list of string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `projects.history`

Answer the one question about version history for a project's folder: keep: true makes the folder a local git repo (no GitHub, no remote) so each session gets its own copy, branch and Undo; keep: false says no and it is never asked again. A folder that already has a history is left as it is. The person, or their agent on their request.

- Input:
  - `keep` boolean, required
  - `project` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `projects.list`

Every project: name, home, folders, people, avatar_seed (what its tile is drawn from), how many threads are in it (picked or by folder), the picked thread ids (picks), newest activity first.

- Input:
  - `archived` boolean
  - `machines` "all" or "local"
- Callers: any caller

### `projects.of`

The project that owns a folder or any folder under it, or null. slug is what the other tools take.

- Input:
  - `cwd` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `projects.reach`

Which projects (and their folders) a caller may reach: the one door core/memory, core/recall and core/files all ask instead of keeping their own copy of this check. caller is the ORIGINAL caller the asking module itself received (ctx.call always relabels the actual meta.caller "module:<name>", so the owner-vs-refused decision below has to be told this explicitly rather than reading it off the call the registry sees); trusted because only a first-party module can reach this tool at all, and that module is the one responsible for forwarding it faithfully. { all: true } for the true owner (its own surfaces, a module, its own session, or an owner device): no restriction. Otherwise { all: false, agent, projects: [{slug, name, folders, threads}] }, deny by default. kind "facts" additionally gives the assistant { all: true } too (personal facts, distilled, not raw content); kind "content" (the default) never does, even for the assistant, which instead gets every project that exists, unconditional and never checked against projects.access (a different privilege tier from a projects: "*" agent, which is checked). A model never asks this on its own behalf: it cannot, callers being module-only.

- Input:
  - `agent` string
  - `caller` string
  - `claim` string or null
  - `kind` "facts" or "content"
  - `person` boolean
  - `thread` string
- Callers: `module`

### `projects.record`

The Project record for a short name: { urn }. Only the agents module asks.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
- Callers: `module`

### `projects.remove-threads`

Remove picks from a project. Threads that ran in the project's folders stay in it by folder, and are listed.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
  - `threads` list of string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `projects.rename`

Rename a project. The slug, folder, threads, teammates and tile stay exactly as they were; only the name changes. Person-only: a model (agent or session) is refused.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `project` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `projects.threads`

The threads in a project, newest first, each saying whether it was picked or ran in the project's folders.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
  - `limit` integer
  - `machines` "all" or "local"
- Callers: any caller

### `projects.watchers.add`

Add watchers to a project: names of people who should hear about its Needs (asks, drafts, merges) without running a session in it. Person-only.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
  - `watchers` list of string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `projects.watchers.remove`

Remove watchers from a project. Person-only.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
  - `watchers` list of string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

## providers

### `providers.list`

Every session provider on this machine, each with its own accounts (several per provider, ADR 0030 phase 2, 0.2 charter minimum 5) and the models it offers. A picker (Chat, the Capsule, the CLI) builds itself from this alone.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

## publish

### `publish.approve`

Approve a preview. The first call asks and holds; a person's decision (publish.decide) completes it, or call again with the task once it is decided.

- Input:
  - `deployment` string, required
  - `space` string
  - `task` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `publish.create`

Start a new site or app as a draft: a name, where its source is, and how it builds. Env entries are secret references, never values.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `source` object, required
  - `approver` string
  - `build` object
  - `env` object
  - `project` string
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `publish.decide`

A person's decision on a held act. Approving completes the act as that person; declining ends it. A model never decides.

- Input:
  - `approve` boolean, required
  - `task` string, required
  - `plan_hash` string
  - `space` string
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `publish.domain.add`

Start connecting a domain to a site. Returns the DNS record to add; nothing serves until it is verified and the site is published.

- Input:
  - `deployment` string, required
  - `host` string, required
  - `canonical` "apex" or "www"
  - `space` string
  - `www` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `publish.domain.remove`

Disconnect a domain from its site.

- Input:
  - `host` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: any caller

### `publish.domain.verify`

Check the DNS record (or the name you own) for a connected domain.

- Input:
  - `host` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `publish.edge`

Write the edge for what is live now: a compose project, a Caddyfile and the Dockerfile of its Caddy image in <home>/publish/<space>/, and the runtime secret files its sites were granted. It does not start anything.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `publish.edge.down`

Stop the edge. Sites stop answering; their files and certificates stay.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `publish.edge.up`

Start the edge: build its Caddy image, copy each live site into its volume and bring the compose project up. Runs publish.edge first. Without a way to start containers on this box it answers not_available and the plan to run by hand.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `publish.flow`

The publish pipeline for a deployment as a Flow definition: build, preview, a person approves, production, rollback.

- Input:
  - `deployment` string, required
  - `kernel` boolean
  - `space` string
- Callers: any caller

### `publish.list`

Every site in the space with its stage and address, newest first. Optional stage or name filter.

- Input:
  - `name` string
  - `space` string
  - `stage` string
- Callers: any caller

### `publish.plan`

What would change if the next step ran: what goes public, at which address, with which secrets, replacing which version.

- Input:
  - `deployment` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: any caller

### `publish.preview`

Build a draft and put it at a private preview address. Refused if a sealed value or one of its own secrets is in the build output.

- Input:
  - `deployment` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `publish.publish`

Put an approved version on the internet. Held for a person every time: the first call asks, a person decides with publish.decide.

- Input:
  - `deployment` string, required
  - `space` string
  - `task` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `publish.retire`

Take a version down and keep its record.

- Input:
  - `deployment` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: any caller

### `publish.rollback`

Put the previous version back. Give the live deployment. Held for a person every time.

- Input:
  - `deployment` string, required
  - `space` string
  - `task` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `publish.secret.grant`

Let one deployment use one vault secret, as an environment name. A real secret is held for a person; nothing is shared with other deployments.

- Input:
  - `deployment` string, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `ref` string, required
  - `space` string
  - `task` string
  - `use` list of "build" or "runtime"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `publish.secret.revoke`

Take a secret away from a deployment and delete its file.

- Input:
  - `deployment` string, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `publish.status`

One site's stage, address, secrets by name, domains and approver. Also says whether the sealed-value check has a ledger behind it. Never a secret value.

- Input:
  - `deployment` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: any caller

## push

### `push.devices`

The devices that get notifications: id, label, push service, when added, last delivered. Never the endpoint or keys.

- Input: none
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `push.key`

The public key a browser subscribes with (applicationServerKey, base64url).

- Input: none
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `push.receipt`

A device showed a test notification: post back its receipt. Known once, for 10 minutes; anything else is unknown_receipt.

- Input:
  - `receipt` string, required
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `push.seen`

A person is using this screen: call it when the screen is shown, when it is hidden, and on the first input after a minute of none. Asks, drafts and watches wait until 3 minutes after the last call; planner rings do not. standalone: the app runs installed (emits push.seen at most once per surface in 10 minutes). device: this screen's push device id (push.subscribe's answer), carried in the event.

- Input:
  - `device` string
  - `standalone` boolean
  - `surface` string
  - `visible` boolean
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `push.settings`

Quiet hours ({start: "22:00", end: "07:00", timezone?}, or null for none), which kinds notify (ask, draft, watch, lesson, planner; lesson is off by default), and planner_label: show a planner item's own words on the lock screen (off by default). With no input, the current settings.

- Input:
  - `kinds` object
  - `planner_label` boolean
  - `quiet` object or null
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `push.subscribe`

Keep this browser's PushSubscription, so the moments you are needed reach this device. Returns the device id.

- Input:
  - `subscription` object, required
  - `label` string
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `push.test`

Send a test notification to every device, or one. Ignores quiet hours. receipt: true puts a one-time receipt in it, which the device posts back (push.receipt) once it is shown.

- Input:
  - `device` string
  - `receipt` boolean
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `push.unsubscribe`

Forget a device: by its id, or by the subscription's endpoint.

- Input:
  - `device` string
  - `endpoint` string
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

## recall

### `recall.eval`

Measure search against a labelled set: MRR and recall for keyword, dense and hybrid, and whether nonsense clears the dense floor.

- Input:
  - `queries` list of object, required
    - `answers` list, required
    - `q` string, required
  - `k` integer
  - `nonsense` list of string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `recall.forget`

Forget these sessions outright: turns, vectors and rows. For memory, when a device's synced sessions are revoked; the files are already gone.

- Input:
  - `sessions` list of string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `recall.index`

Index new and changed transcripts now. Returns what the pass did.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `recall.links`

The turns that touched something, newest first: a file (ref is a path or just its name; kind file for changes, read for reads, both by default), a commit (kind commit, a short or full hash), or a url (kind url). Each is a pointer (session:seq) for recall.turn, with a snippet.

- Input:
  - `ref` string, required
  - `agent` string
  - `kind` one of "file", "read", "commit", "url"
  - `limit` integer
  - `session` string
  - `since` integer: ms since epoch
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `recall.pointers`

For a rollover (Vyre's own, when a session's window fills): the windows of one thread (session ids, oldest first) indexed now, then the one split Vyre's seed makes of them: the last turns, newest window first, whose text adds up to tail_chars, word for word (a long turn cut, with the pointer that reads it whole), and, for every turn before them, an index of pointers: the person's own requests as lines (session:turn, who, when, the start of what was said), the files touched and the commits made. Pointers read back with recall.turn. A person's surface or a module asks, for the sessions of a thread it runs; never an agent.

- Input:
  - `sessions` list of string, required
  - `lines` integer
  - `tail_chars` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `recall.related`

1 to 3 of a project's own past sessions relevant to what the person is about to say, for chat's "From your past sessions" hint while they type. Each hit is one turn (its own session, seq, role, ts, name, cwd and a short snippet), the person's own or the assistant's; chat/native-core render the reason sentence and the link. Owner surfaces only, and only inside a real, mapped project: project_cwds must name at least one folder that is actually a project's; an ad-hoc or unmapped folder gets no hint rather than the whole corpus.

- Input:
  - `project_cwds` list of string, required
  - `text` string, required
  - `limit` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `recall.sealscan`

One look at what Recall's index already holds that has the shape of a sealed value (an SSN, a card or bank number, an IBAN and the rest): which table and column, how many rows and which classes, and how many search vectors were made from them, never a value. It changes nothing. New turns are scrubbed on the way in.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `recall.sealscrub`

Rewrite what Recall's index already holds that has the shape of a sealed value: each matched span becomes a placeholder, nothing else in a turn, title or name changes, and the search vectors made from a changed turn are dropped and made again. Only the person, with presence. One log row (counts and classes) is kept.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `recall.search`

Search every Claude Code session on this machine for turns about something. Returns the best turns with their session's name, title and folder.

- Input:
  - `q` string, required
  - `agent` string
  - `hybrid` boolean
  - `limit` integer
  - `links` list of object: keep only turns that touched these (a file path or name, a commit hash, a url), or sit next to one that did
    - `ref` string, required
    - `kind` one of "file", "read", "commit", "url"
  - `machines` "all" or "local"
  - `per_session` integer
  - `prefix` boolean: each word as a prefix, all of them, keyword only: for completion while typing
  - `project_cwds` list of string
  - `role` "user" or "assistant"
  - `sessions` list of string: also these sessions wherever they ran (a project's attached sessions); from modules and the person's surfaces only
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `recall.sessions`

Indexed sessions, newest first, optionally only those in or under a folder, since a time, started by a person, or with the given ids.

- Input:
  - `agent` string
  - `cwd` string
  - `human` boolean
  - `ids` list of string
  - `limit` integer
  - `machines` "all" or "local"
  - `since` number
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `recall.setup`

Install the search model now (the library and its weights, once) and load it, so search ranks by meaning. Resolves when it is ready or has failed, and says which.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `recall.status`

How much is indexed, when the last pass ran, and whether search can rank by meaning.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `recall.thread`

One session and its turns, in order. Takes a session id or an unambiguous prefix of one.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
  - `agent` string
  - `from` integer
  - `limit` integer
  - `machines` "all" or "local"
  - `source` "box" or "mac"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `recall.transcript`

A rich read of one session for a person's own screen: what was said, thinking, every tool call with its input and output, and each turn's time and tokens. Takes a session id or an unambiguous prefix of one. Without from, the last blocks; before pages back.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
  - `before` integer
  - `from` integer
  - `limit` integer
  - `machines` "all" or "local"
  - `source` "box" or "mac"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `recall.turn`

A span of one past session, word for word: the turns themselves, no summary, each with its pointer (session:seq), its time, and what it touched (files, commits, urls). Name the turn with seq, and before and after for the turns around it, or give from with to or span. A turn the search index had to cut is read whole from the transcript. Redacted like everything Recall holds.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required: a session id, or an unambiguous prefix of one
  - `after` integer
  - `agent` string
  - `before` integer
  - `from` integer
  - `full` boolean: false: give a long turn as the index holds it (cut) instead of reading the transcript
  - `seq` integer
  - `span` integer
  - `to` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `recall.unwatch`

Stop following a session (a watch id from recall.watch).

- Input:
  - `watch` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `recall.watch`

Follow one session live: each completed turn arrives as a session.turn event (thread = the session id) and session.state says whether a reply is under way. from is a turn id to replay after first; without it, only new turns. Call again with the same watch id to renew it: a watch nobody renews ends after 3 minutes, and one whose session is quiet for 30 minutes ends too.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
  - `from` string
  - `watch` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

## records

### `records.actors`

The people of a Space: who each is and the role they hold. Only what the caller's role may see.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.create`

Make a record of a type.

- Input:
  - `data` object, required
  - `type` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.define`

Add or change record types and their fields (a DefineDiff). The kernel decides who may.

- Input:
  - `diff` object, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.dev-seed`

DEVELOPMENT ONLY, while the presence stand-in is on: seed a Space with the walk's types, records and tasks. Otherwise it does nothing.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: `cli`, `local`

### `records.events`

The Space's event log as the caller may read it: for a record, for a task, or all; newest last.

- Input:
  - `limit` integer
  - `record` string
  - `since` integer
  - `space` string
  - `task` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.forget`

Forget one record for good: the text of every task about it is emptied, the store destroys it and its history, its events lose their data, and its sealed values are destroyed. Linked files stay in Files. Cannot be undone. Customize-level (admin and owner), the person's own act with their presence; an assistant proposes it with records.forget.propose.

- Input:
  - `urn` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`
- Needs a person present.

### `records.forget.propose`

Ask the person to forget one record for good. Nothing is forgotten: a task goes to the person, who does it themselves with their presence. For an assistant, which may only propose.

- Input:
  - `urn` string, required
  - `why` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.get`

One record by its reference, or null when it is not there or not yours to see.

- Input:
  - `urn` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.holders`

Who holds one role: a page of role records, each naming its holder. `role` is a role type, `stage` narrows to one stage. Ended roles are left out unless `include_ended` is true. Only rows the caller may read.

- Input:
  - `role` string, required
  - `cursor` string
  - `include_ended` boolean
  - `limit` integer
  - `space` string
  - `stage` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.kits.get`

One Kit from the library in the form the Flows tools take (flows.kit.card to see what it would do, flows.kit.propose to ask for the install).

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.kits.library`

The Kits this build ships, before anything is installed: id, name, version, a plain description and what each adds (types, templates, roles, flows, views, sealed fields).

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.linked`

Everything that links to a record (the reverse of a link field), across types, only what the caller may read. `truncated` is true when more exist than `limit` (default 50, at most 200).

- Input:
  - `urn` string, required
  - `field` string
  - `limit` integer
  - `type` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.list`

One page of records of a type: filter, sort, a cursor from the last page. include_deleted lists the removed ones too (the Bin): your own, or all for an owner or admin.

- Input:
  - `type` string, required
  - `cursor` string
  - `filter` any
  - `include_deleted` boolean
  - `limit` integer
  - `sort` list
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.me`

The person making this call, and the Space it acts in.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.reference`

A record put in front of the AI (the # in the composer): the record as data, with every sealed part (and any part this room may not read) a {{field:urn#name}} placeholder, never a value. Null when it is not there or not yours to see.

- Input:
  - `urn` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.reveal`

Show a sealed value to the person on their own screen, once. Human-only: the person's presence proof rides beside the request and the chain must be exactly one person.

- Input:
  - `field` string, required
  - `purpose` string, required
  - `urn` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.roles`

What a contact or organization is to the Space: every role record that points at it (prospect, client, ambassador, whatever the Space defined), current ones first. Ended roles are included unless `include_ended` is false. Only roles the caller may read.

- Input:
  - `urn` string, required
  - `include_ended` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.seal-put`

Put a value into a record's sealed field. It goes straight to the sealing process and never rides the record; the record keeps only the reference. The person's own act.

- Input:
  - `field` string, required
  - `urn` string, required
  - `value` string, required
  - `class` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.sees-as`

The record as the person sees it, or as their assistant would (sealed fields only as placeholders, and only the fields its grants allow).

- Input:
  - `urn` string, required
  - `who` "person" or "assistant", required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.types`

The record types of a Space, as defined (a type may carry kind: project). The kernel's own bookkeeping types are left out unless `system: true` is asked for, and then carry system: true.

- Input:
  - `space` string
  - `system` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.update`

Change a record's fields; a stale base_version is refused (version_conflict).

- Input:
  - `base_version` integer, required
  - `patch` object, required
  - `urn` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `records.workspace.create`

Internal, for the spaces module: confirm a new Space has its store. The built-in store is always there.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `name` string
  - `store` string
- Callers: `module`

### `records.workspace.delete`

Internal, for the spaces module: a Space's store goes with the Space, never by this call.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: `module`

## relay

### `relay.apply`

Turn the relay on at a signed instruction from the owner's app. Only the wink module calls it, after verifying the signature.

- Input:
  - `url` string
- Callers: any caller

### `relay.code.alloc`

Ask the relay for a free typed-code rendezvous for this box (5 minutes, one at a time). Answers { rv, exp }, or null when the relay is busy, away or has no typed codes. Modules only.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.code.release`

Give the typed-code rendezvous back. Modules only.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.code.reply`

Answer a typed code's message (the relay.code-asked event): q is its id, m the reply in base64url, or none to refuse. Modules only.

- Input:
  - `q` string, required
  - `m` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.device.info`

A paired relay device as the link module's companion check needs it: kind, trusted, when it paired, its presence key id and whether it was removed. Null for an id never paired. Modules only.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.device.presence`

The presence key id enrolled for a paired relay device, or null.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.devices.admit-server`

Admit a paired server's relay key as a row of kind server (never an app device): it may open one peer stream, and the home's door lets it read the network's status. `pub` is the 32-byte key, base64url; `server` is its Wink device id. Modules only. Answers { id }.

- Input:
  - `pub` string, required
  - `server` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.devices.all`

The paired computers and phones, for a module: id, name, kind and whether each is connected now.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `relay.devices.ask-trust`

A browser paired from the hosted web app asks the owner to trust it fully. Only that browser, about itself; it tells every surface once (device.trust-asked) and the owner's own relay.devices.trust, with presence, is the approval.

- Input: none
- Callers: `web`

### `relay.devices.clear-leftover`

On a server nobody owns: let go of every paired device row left by a pairing that never completed ownership, so a new owner's pairing is not refused by them. Only the Wink module asks, and only while the server is unowned. Answers { cleared }.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `relay.devices.drop`

Close a paired device's connections and refuse it from now on, for a module that has just removed it for the owner.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `relay.devices.drop-server`

Take a paired server's relay row away and close its channels. Modules only. Answers { dropped }.

- Input:
  - `server` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.devices.list`

Devices paired through the relay: id, name, when paired and last seen, whether presence is enrolled, and whether it is connected now.

- Input: none
- Callers: `web`

### `relay.devices.node`

A paired relay device's own Noise identity and, if it has reported one, its tailnet node, for a module to check ownership or open a direct connection, never for a person or a device to call about itself.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.devices.path`

A paired device says which way it reaches the box now (relay or direct over the tailnet) and its measured round trip. Over the tailnet the first report carries the device id and the one-time code the relay path gave it, which links the device to its tailnet node.

- Input:
  - `path` "relay" or "direct", required
  - `code` string
  - `id` string
  - `rtt` number
- Callers: any caller

### `relay.devices.remove`

Remove a paired device: its connections close at once and it can no longer reach the box through the relay. Its presence key is removed too.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.devices.rename`

Rename a paired device.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `relay.devices.trust`

Give a browser paired from the hosted web app the full powers of the owner's app (pairing devices, vault secrets), or take them back. Not callable from a web device that is not trusted.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `trusted` boolean, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.disable`

Turn the relay off: the box stops connecting out, and every device connected through it is dropped. Paired devices stay paired.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.enable`

Turn the relay on: the box connects out to the relay so paired devices can reach it from anywhere.

- Input:
  - `url` string
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.join`

This Vyre becomes a device of another box, redeeming a one-time pairing code minted there (relay.pair.start or onboard.join{action:"relay"}). One redemption: the channel closes once paired, then this tool returns what the other box said (its name, this device's id, whether presence enrolled). becomeDevice, when true, flips this machine to "device" once paired (onboard.machine): the same flag onboard.join{action:"verify",becomeDevice} takes. Does not keep a connection open; that is not built yet. A pasted URL that is not a real Vyre pairing code is refused before any prompt. Not available on a Mac yet: see vyre-core (ADR 0040).

- Input:
  - `url` string, required
  - `becomeDevice` boolean
  - `name` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.pair.first`

During onboarding only, before this box has any person on a device: make the QR code for the first device. Refused once a device is paired or a tailnet owner exists.

- Input: none
- Callers: `onboard`
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.pair.pending.confirm`

The yes for a waiting pairing (a gated ticket, X-1): enrols the device that redeemed it, now. Only the Wink module, which has had the person pick the right three words. With `trusted` the device is marked trusted at the same moment (an owner-confirmed phone or computer). Answers { paired, id, key?, alg? }: the public key the device offered in its hello, so the pairing can bind its session to it.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `trusted` boolean
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.pair.start`

Make a QR code that pairs one more device with this box through the relay. The code works once, for 10 minutes; making a new one voids the last.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.pair.ticket`

Mint a one-time pairing ticket for the Vyre code (Wink): a phone that scans it resolves the box's identity from the relay, then pairs exactly as relay.pair.start's QR does. Works once, for 5 minutes; call again for a fresh one (an old, unused ticket is simply left to expire, unlike relay.pair.start's single live QR). Not available on a Mac yet: see vyre-core (ADR 0040).

- Input:
  - `seed` string
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.pair.window.close`

Close the pairing window: the live code is withdrawn at the relay.

- Input:
  - `window` string, required
- Callers: `deck`, `tailnet`

### `relay.pair.window.confirm`

The person confirms the phone that is pairing (shown as pairing.requested, with its name and key fingerprint). Only then is it enrolled, and the window closes. Answers { confirmed: true }.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `window` string, required
- Callers: `deck`, `tailnet`

### `relay.pair.window.open`

Open a pairing window on this screen: one proof, then up to 10 minutes of Wink codes this screen may renew without another prompt (relay.pair.window.renew). Answers { window, closesAt, pingEveryMs, ticket, ticketExpiresAt }. The window is for pair.device on this box only. A phone that redeems a code is enrolled only after relay.pair.window.confirm.

- Input: none
- Callers: `deck`, `tailnet`
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.pair.window.ping`

The open screen's heartbeat for its pairing window, every 15 seconds. The window closes after 30 seconds of silence. Answers { closesInMs }.

- Input:
  - `window` string, required
- Callers: `deck`, `tailnet`

### `relay.pair.window.reject`

"Not you?": refuse the phone that is waiting to be confirmed, shown as pairing.requested. The window stays open and the slot is free at once, so a stranger who redeemed a code cannot hold it; the screen renews the code for the real phone. Answers { rejected: true }.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `window` string, required
- Callers: `deck`, `tailnet`

### `relay.pair.window.renew`

Renew the Wink code inside an open pairing window, with no new proof. It withdraws the previous code at the relay first, so one is ever live. Only the screen that opened the window, while it keeps pinging; at most one every 15 seconds and 40 in a window. Answers { ticket, ticketExpiresAt }.

- Input:
  - `window` string, required
- Callers: `deck`, `tailnet`

### `relay.route.id`

This box's route id and route public key (base64url), for signing into the name directory, and the box's own public key (`box`), which the Wink module hashes into the words a pairing shows. Modules only.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.route.sign`

Sign a name-directory request with the route key. Only a message that begins vyre-names-v1, a newline and this box's own route is signed. Modules only.

- Input:
  - `message` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.setup.begin`

Start a setup session from the code on the install line: register the sealed offer at the relay, discard any earlier unclaimed setup session and its device, and start the hour. Modules only.

- Input:
  - `code` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.setup.end`

End the setup session: drop the setup device, its presence key and its channel. Called at the claim, and by the session itself when its hour runs out with no claim. Modules only, never callable through the setup channel.

- Input:
  - `reason` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.setup.status`

Where the setup session is: none, waiting for the page, paired, or contested (another server used the code first), whether the relay holds the offer, whether the one pairing ticket is made, when the hour ends, and the four check words the page shows too.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `relay.status`

Whether the relay is on and connected, which relay, this box's route id, and how many paired devices and open connections it has.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `relay.ticket.mint`

A Wink ticket with an offer sealed into its record (an invitation: kind, role, projects), or a ticket from a seed both ends derived (a typed code's key). With `gate` (phone or server) the redemption makes nothing but a waiting pairing until relay.pair.pending.confirm says yes (X-1). Modules only; answers like relay.pair.ticket.

- Input:
  - `gate` "phone" or "server"
  - `offer` object
  - `seed` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `relay.web.pin`

Pin the hosted web app to one release this box knows, or clear the pin (empty release) to follow the newest one.

- Input:
  - `release` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `relay.web.release`

Which build of the hosted web app this box trusts: its release, the content-addressed folder sha and the manifest hash the loader must check. The owner's pin, or the newest release this box knows.

- Input: none
- Callers: `web`

## releases

### `releases.cert`

The SHA-256 of the owner's Android release certificate, made on first use. `vyre phone add` pins it.

- Input: none
- Callers: `agent`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `releases.sign`

Sign the Android release now in the release folder (CI's unsigned APK named by android.json) with the owner's key, so the first download is instant. `vyre update` calls it after it places a new build. Idempotent.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `module`

## rules

### `rules.accept`

Make a proposal a standing rule, exactly as proposed. An owner's act, with their presence.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.define`

Make a standing rule. An owner's act, with their presence. A rule only tightens: it can refuse, make an act wait for a named approver, or allow a draft only; it never allows anything.

- Input:
  - `rule` object, required: { kind: never | draft_only | always_ask, binds: [assistants | members], covers: { actions: [exact action names], resource?: urn pattern }, approver?: { person } | { role } (always_ask only), label }
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.disable`

Turn a rule off without deleting it: it binds nothing until it is turned on. An owner's act, with their presence.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.dismiss`

Turn a proposal down. An owner's act, with their presence.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.enable`

Turn a switched-off rule back on. An owner's act, with their presence.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.get`

One rule or proposal by id, with its plain-words view.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.list`

The Space's standing rules (on and off) and the proposals waiting for an owner, each with a plain-words view. A manager and above.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.propose`

Suggest a rule. It does nothing until an owner accepts it.

- Input:
  - `rule` object, required: { kind: never | draft_only | always_ask, binds: [assistants | members], covers: { actions: [exact action names], resource?: urn pattern }, approver?: { person } | { role } (always_ask only), label }
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.remove`

Delete a standing rule. An owner's act, with their presence.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `rules.test`

What the rules would do to an act, without doing it. `as` is assistant (default), member or assistant_for_member; `action` is the act and `resource` its urn. `id` tries one stored rule alone (on or off); `rule` tries one nobody has defined yet beside the ones in force. Answers { outcome: never | always_ask | draft_only | none, binds: [{ id, kind, label, status, view }] }.

- Input:
  - `action` string, required
  - `as` one of "assistant", "member", "assistant_for_member"
  - `id` string
  - `resource` string
  - `rule` object: { kind: never | draft_only | always_ask, binds: [assistants | members], covers: { actions: [exact action names], resource?: urn pattern }, approver?: { person } | { role } (always_ask only), label }
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

## runner

### `runner.lock`

Close the workspace on this computer. The data stays encrypted.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.move`

Move a session to the space's server, after a last checkpoint here.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.place`

Where a session in this space would run now: here, the server or waiting, with the reason in words.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `pinned` boolean
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.places`

Where the chats of a space run: the sessions lent to a computer, each with its chat, the computer's name and whether it is connected. Only your own chats.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.recover`

Put a session on this server back to its last whole turn before it is resumed after an unclean stop: the provider's transcript is rewritten to exactly the sealed lines (a torn last line and an unfinished turn are dropped). Answers the sealed turn and its state, or nothing when the session was never sealed. Call it only while no process of the session is running.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.revoke`

The home says this computer's grant for a space ended: stop its sessions and delete the local work and keys.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.start`

Start a session here. The space's own definition of the session decides the program, the routes and the credentials it may use; the caller names only the space and the session. Needs both grants and a held key lease.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `chat` string: The chat this work belongs to (chat_<id>), so the chat can say where it runs.
  - `resume` boolean
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.status`

Whether this computer can run a space's sessions, and what is running here.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `runner.stop`

Stop a session running here.

- Input:
  - `session` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

## screen

### `screen.context`

What is on this Mac's screen right now: the front app (name, bundle, pid), its window (title, frame), the focused control (role, name, value, selected text, frame), the page URL in a browser, and the visible text of the window in reading order (capped at textMax characters; truncated says it was cut). Redacted: in a password field or while secure input is on, the focused value and selection are left out and secure is true, and password fields never appear in the text. In a password manager, a system sign-in or permission dialog, a security pane of System Settings, or a Vyre surface, only the app and window title come back, with blind naming why. Local callers only.

- Input:
  - `text` boolean: Include the visible text. Default true; false is faster.
  - `textMax` integer: Most characters of visible text, 0-20000. Default 4000.
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `screen.shot`

Take a screenshot now, of the front window (default) or the main display (window: false). Returns the path of a PNG in a private folder that is deleted after 60 seconds, plus its width and height; read the file to see it. Needs the Screen Recording grant. Refused, with blind naming why, where screen.context is blind. Local callers only.

- Input:
  - `window` boolean: Only the front window. Default true.
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

## sessions

### `sessions.accounts.add`

Add an account: a label, its kind, and for an api-key or setup-token the vault item that already holds its credential (add it in the Vault first and grant it to threads; this never touches its value). kind login has no vault item: the provider's own sign-in fills that account's private home. scope is { projects: "*"|[slugs], agents: "*"|[names] }, default "*" (every project and agent may use it until it is bound narrower). is_default makes it the provider's pick when nothing else resolves. Each account runs as its own user on a server, so one account's sign-in is unreadable from another's.

- Input:
  - `label` string, required
  - `provider` string, required
  - `is_default` boolean
  - `kind` one of "api-key", "setup-token", "login"
  - `scope` object
    - `agents` any
    - `projects` any
  - `vault_item` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `sessions.accounts.bind`

Grant an account to one more project or agent (added to its scope, others it already has kept), or make it its provider's default.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `agent` string
  - `is_default` boolean
  - `project` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `sessions.accounts.key`

Add an AI account from an API key: kind openai-compatible (key and base_url), anthropic-compatible (key and base_url) or openrouter (key). The key is checked with one cheap call, stored in the Vault (bound to that address) and never returned or shown again; the key is sent only to that host, which is looked up again on every turn and refused if it points at a private, tailnet or metadata address. Removing the account removes the key. Only the person (or their own words) adds one.

- Input:
  - `key` string, required
  - `kind` one of "openai-compatible", "anthropic-compatible", "openrouter", required
  - `base_url` string
  - `label` string
  - `model` string
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.accounts.list`

Every account on a provider, or every account on every provider. Each names a vault item (never a value) and its scope: which projects and agents it is granted to.

- Input:
  - `provider` string
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.accounts.remove`

Remove an account. Threads already resumed on it keep running; the next resume on that thread asks for another (a removed account is never a silent fallback).

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.accounts.resolve`

Which account a session on this provider uses, for a project/agent/explicit choice, scope-checked either way.

- Input:
  - `provider` string, required
  - `account` string
  - `agent` string
  - `project` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.accounts.set`

Set an account's privacy choice (Grok only): privacy true is privacy mode on, xAI does not keep the account's sessions and Grok cannot make video; false is off, xAI keeps them and may train on them, and Grok can make video. Records the choice; the setting itself is changed on xAI's side, in Grok's /privacy settings.

- Input:
  - `account` string, required
  - `privacy` boolean, required
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.accounts.signin`

Sign an account in with its provider's own login (Codex --device-auth, Grok Build's device code, Claude's login), no token pasted or copied. Start: { provider, label? } makes a login account (or { account } for one that exists) and answers { flow, step: "code", url, code } to show; the person approves on any browser. Then { flow } says waiting, done or failed; for a login that wants a code back ({ step: "url", paste: true }) send { flow, code }. The token is written by the provider's own command into that account's private home; Vyre never reads it.

- Input:
  - `account` string
  - `code` string
  - `flow` string
  - `label` string
  - `provider` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `sessions.environment`

The environment brief an agent starting now is told (what Vyre is, its Space, its records, how to work, approvals, memory, what it can reach), built from live reads and cut to a budget. The same text goes to every model and driver.

- Input:
  - `agent` string
  - `agent_kind` string
  - `artifacts_dir` string
  - `contacts` list of object
  - `now` number
  - `project` string
  - `provider` string
  - `space_zone` string
  - `zone` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.files.read`

Read one regular file from inside an account's own folder, as that account (`file` is relative to the account's HOME, under a provider's own folder such as .grok or .codex, no links, at most 20 MB). Answers { size, sha256, data_b64 }. Not a public name.

- Input:
  - `account` string, required
  - `file` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.limits.get`

The concurrency limits: box-wide (sessions.limits in config: max_active_teammates, max_subagents) and a project's own.

- Input:
  - `project` string
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.limits.set`

Set a project's limits: at most this many active teammates (max_active) and subagents across all its sessions (max_subagents). null removes one. Over a limit, new ones wait their turn.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
  - `max_active` integer or null
  - `max_subagents` integer or null
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `sessions.mention.resolve`

What an account tagged with @ means for a thread: the person asked this one turn to run on it. No grant; threads.send reads the tag. Called by the mentions core.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `said` string
  - `thread` string
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.mention.search`

The @ picker's Accounts results: the AI accounts that are signed in (Claude, Codex, Grok, OpenRouter), by provider name. Names and a short hint only.

- Input:
  - `limit` integer
  - `q` string
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.mode.get`

The permission mode new sessions in a project start in (sessions.mode.set), or null for Claude Code's default (ask).

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.mode.resolve`

The mode a new session in a project starts in, for the Switchboard.

- Input:
  - `project` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.mode.set`

The permission mode new sessions in a project start in: default (ask), acceptEdits, plan or bypassPermissions ("Doesn't ask": the security floor and the Gate still hold; only sessions with Vyre's plugin take it).
