The person's own; default (or no mode) clears it. A running session keeps its mode (threads.mode changes that).

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
  - `mode` one of "default", "acceptEdits", "plan", "bypassPermissions"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `sessions.models`

The models a thread can switch to (threads.model): id and label, the aliases Claude Code takes first.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.models.get`

What each kind of session runs on: the model per purpose (chat, agent, project, teammate, capsule, job, memory, planner, learn, helper) and per project, and where each comes from. An agent's own model (agents.update) wins over these. aliases is the list of model aliases to offer, with a label and a line each.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.models.resolve`

The model a session starting now runs on, and where that comes from.

- Input:
  - `model` string
  - `project` string
  - `purpose` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.models.set`

Set the model for a purpose (purpose:<chat|agent|project|teammate|capsule|job|memory|planner|learn|helper>) or a project (project:<slug>): an alias (opus, sonnet, haiku) or a full model id. model null removes the override. Applies from the next session.

- Input:
  - `scope` string, required
  - `model` string or null
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `sessions.prompt.compose`

The system prompt for a session starting now: the environment brief, then the levels around Vyre's own launch text, then the project's own context (context, for a driver with no SessionStart hook). purpose "capsule" is the Capsule's quick answer (Vyre IQ): the whole prompt, with append read as its facts.

- Input:
  - `agent` string
  - `agent_kind` string
  - `append` string
  - `artifacts_dir` string
  - `contacts` list of object
  - `context` string
  - `facts` list of string
  - `now` number
  - `project` string
  - `provider` string
  - `purpose` string
  - `space_zone` string
  - `zone` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.prompt.get`

The system prompt set at one level (assistant, agent:<name> or project:<slug>): its text, mode (append or replace) and version, or null when nothing is set.

- Input:
  - `scope` string, required: assistant, agent:<name>, project:<slug> or capsule (the Capsule's quick answer, Vyre IQ)
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.prompt.history`

Every version of the system prompt at one level, newest first.

- Input:
  - `scope` string, required: assistant, agent:<name>, project:<slug> or capsule (the Capsule's quick answer, Vyre IQ)
  - `limit` integer
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.prompt.preview`

The system prompt a session would start with, for an agent and a project: the levels used and how they combine. Without Vyre's own launch text, which the Switchboard adds. purpose "capsule": the Capsule's quick answer (Vyre IQ) with no facts.

- Input:
  - `agent` string
  - `agent_kind` string
  - `project` string
  - `purpose` "capsule"
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.prompt.revert`

Undo edits: make an older version of a level's system prompt the current one again (as a new version, so the revert can be undone too).

- Input:
  - `scope` string, required: assistant, agent:<name>, project:<slug> or capsule (the Capsule's quick answer, Vyre IQ)
  - `version` integer, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `sessions.prompt.set`

Set the system prompt at one level. mode "append" (the default) adds it after Claude Code's own; "replace" makes it the whole system prompt: Replacing the system prompt drops Claude Code's own instructions (tool use, safety and coding behaviour), so it is for experts only. Every edit is a new version; sessions.prompt.revert undoes one. Applies from the next session. Empty text in append mode clears the level. At most 20000 characters.

- Input:
  - `scope` string, required: assistant, agent:<name>, project:<slug> or capsule (the Capsule's quick answer, Vyre IQ)
  - `text` string, required
  - `mode` "append" or "replace"
  - `note` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `sessions.providers.learn`

Record what a provider reported in a session: the models its account can use (id, label) and its plan. For providers.list; not a public name.

- Input:
  - `provider` string, required
  - `account` string
  - `models` list of object
    - `id` string, required
    - `label` string
  - `plan` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.providers.snapshot`

Every session provider this module speaks for (claude, codex, grok), each with its own accounts and the models it offers. For providers.list (core/providers) to assemble; not a public name itself.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.routes.get`

The fallback order for a scope (default, project:<slug> or agent:<name>): the ordered (provider, account) list a thread moves down when its turn hits a limit. Without a scope, every list.

- Input:
  - `scope` string
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.routes.next`

The next (provider, account) a limited thread moves to, for the Switchboard. Not a public name.

- Input:
  - `provider` string, required
  - `account` string
  - `agent` string
  - `project` string
  - `tried` list of string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.routes.set`

Set the fallback order for a scope: entries is an ordered list of { provider, account? }, e.g. Claude, then Codex, then Grok. An empty list clears it. Two entries on one provider (two accounts combining one vendor's quota) may break that vendor's terms: it saves only with acknowledge: true, after the person has seen the warning. An agent sets only its own list or a project it is granted.

- Input:
  - `entries` list of object, required
    - `provider` string, required
    - `account` string
    - `model` string: The model this entry runs (required for OpenRouter): what the person chose.
  - `scope` string, required
  - `acknowledge` boolean
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.setup`

Install the Claude Agent SDK now (it installs itself on first use otherwise) and wait. Says how much it downloads. Sessions use it from the next one on.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `sessions.slots`

The concurrency ledger (for the Switchboard and teammates): take a teammate or subagent slot (waiting its turn, or not), release one, release all an owner holds, or read what is held.

- Input:
  - `action` one of "take", "release", "release-owner", "status", required
  - `auth` string: The credential the new session runs on: its plan's usage pause applies.
  - `id` string
  - `key` string
  - `kind` "teammate" or "subagent"
  - `owner` string
  - `project` string
  - `timeout_ms` integer
  - `wait` boolean
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.slots.status`

How many teammates and subagents are running and waiting, box-wide and per project, and the limits in force.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.status`

How the sessions Vyre starts run on this machine: the driver (sdk or cli), the Claude credential they use (login, setup-token or api-key), which Claude Code, the idle close and the cap, and whether the Agent SDK is installed.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.usage.get`

Each Claude credential's plan usage as Claude Code last reported it (status, window, utilization, resets_at), and whether new teammates and subagents on it are paused.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `sessions.usage.report`

The Switchboard's report of a credential's plan usage (thread.limit).

- Input:
  - `auth` string, required
  - `status` string, required
  - `kind` string
  - `resets_at` number
  - `utilization` number
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sessions.usage.resume`

Resume anyway: start teammates and subagents on this credential again although its plan is near the limit, until the window resets.

- Input:
  - `auth` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

## settings

### `settings.changes`

Recent settings changes, newest first: {id, key, level, target, by, said, at, undone}. Give key for one setting's history.

- Input:
  - `key` string
  - `limit` number
- Callers: any caller

### `settings.get`

Settings with the value in effect and where it comes from (session, device, project, account, default). Give key for one, group for a group, nothing for all; project, device and session to see that view (device defaults to the caller's own). A secret setting's values are masked for anyone but the person.

- Input:
  - `device` string
  - `group` string
  - `key` string
  - `project` string
  - `session` string
- Callers: any caller

### `settings.request`

Change or reset a setting because the person asked you to in this conversation (for example "use Sonnet by default in this project"). It works only when the person's own words asked for this change; otherwise it is refused and you should tell them they can change it in Settings or ask you directly. Give value to set it, or reset: true to clear it. The person sees who changed it and can undo it.

- Input:
  - `key` string, required
  - `device` string
  - `level` one of "account", "project", "device", "session"
  - `project` string
  - `reset` boolean
  - `session` string
  - `value` any
- Callers: `mcp`

### `settings.reset`

Remove a setting's value at one level, so the next level down (then the default) applies again. Logged and undoable like settings.set.

- Input:
  - `key` string, required
  - `confirm` boolean
  - `device` string
  - `level` one of "account", "project", "device", "session"
  - `preview` boolean
  - `project` string
  - `session` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `settings.resolve`

The Vyre-owned values a session starting now in this project should use, as {key: value}. Unset keys are left out.

- Input:
  - `project` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `settings.schema`

Every setting the running modules declare: key, owning module, group, label, type and choices (a module may name them at run time), the levels it may be set at (account, project, device, session), when a change applies (live, next session, restart), whether Claude Code's own files hold it (owner C), and whether changing it loosens security (a proof) or needs a confirm. hub says where the hub file is and its rev.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `settings.set`

Change a setting at account level, or for one project, device or session (give it). The value is checked against the setting's type, and by its module when it names a check. preview: true returns what would change and writes nothing, with confirm naming what it widens or loosens. No confirm step and no proof: every change is logged (settings.changes) and can be undone (settings.undo). Returns the value now in effect.

- Input:
  - `key` string, required
  - `value` any, required
  - `confirm` boolean
  - `device` string
  - `level` one of "account", "project", "device", "session"
  - `preview` boolean
  - `project` string
  - `session` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `settings.snapshot`

Every setting's value in effect for one surface, in one read: {rev, device, values: {key: value}, sources: {key: level}, levels: {key: {account?, project?, device?, session?}}}. device defaults to the caller's own and is echoed. Compare rev after a reconnect; follow settings.changed after that. A secret setting's values are masked for anyone but the person.

- Input:
  - `device` string
  - `project` string
  - `session` string
- Callers: any caller

### `settings.undo`

Undo one settings change (its id from settings.changed or settings.changes): the value before it comes back at the same level. No confirm and no proof.

- Input:
  - `change` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `settings.write`

A module sets or clears one of its own settings (kept by Vyre, no confirm, not loosening security). Modules only.

- Input:
  - `key` string, required
  - `level` "account" or "project"
  - `project` string
  - `value` any
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

## sidebar

### `sidebar.edit`

Change the caller's OWN sidebar by one step. op is add (what: a place's or screen's name, or entry), remove, hide, show, move (before: another entry's key, or index), group (group: a name, or null), set (entries: the whole list, from the app's drag and drop) or reset (drop my own list; for sidebar.edit only). "Put Documents in my sidebar" is { op: "add", what: "Documents" }. The Space's default is sidebar.team.

- Input:
  - `op` one of "add", "remove", "hide", "show", "move", "group", "set", "reset", required
  - `before` string
  - `entries` list
  - `entry` any
  - `group` string or null
  - `index` number
  - `key` string
  - `space` string
  - `what` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `sidebar.get`

The sidebar for a Space: the Space's default (null when none is stored, then the built-in places stand), the caller's own list, the two merged, whether the caller may set the default, and the installed module screens that can be added.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

### `sidebar.serve`

A team member's sidebar call, run on behalf of the person and the role the Space's kernel stated. Called only by the home's own sidebar service.

- Input:
  - `call` one of "get", "edit", "team", required
  - `input` object, required
  - `person` string, required
  - `role` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `sidebar.stored`

The stored lists behind the settings sidebar.default and sidebar.mine. Called by the settings hub as its store: with no value it reads { value }, with a value it keeps it (cleaned) and answers { value }.

- Input:
  - `key` "default" or "mine", required
  - `value` any
- Callers: any caller

### `sidebar.team`

Change the Space's DEFAULT sidebar by one step (the same ops as sidebar.edit). Only the Space's owner or admin role may; an assistant's request is held for their yes first.

- Input:
  - `op` one of "add", "remove", "hide", "show", "move", "group", "set", "reset", required
  - `before` string
  - `entries` list
  - `entry` any
  - `group` string or null
  - `index` number
  - `key` string
  - `space` string
  - `what` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `harness`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `tailnet`

## sideview

### `sideview.close`

Put back the windows the side view moved, where they were before sideview.open. Local callers only.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `sideview.open`

Tile this Mac for working beside a session: the session's window on the left (ratio of the display's width, 0.29 by default, full height between the menu bar and the Dock) and Chrome's front window filling the rest, edge to edge. session: "front" (the front terminal, the default), "terminal" (the top terminal window), {bundle} or {pid}. browser: "chrome" (default) or "glass" (the paired box's Glass page for `glass`, an agent's name or box). url opens in a new Chrome tab. Chrome is opened if it has no window. The frames the windows had are remembered for sideview.close. Needs the Accessibility grant. Local callers only.

- Input:
  - `browser` "chrome" or "glass"
  - `glass` string: With browser glass: an agent's name, or box (the default).
  - `panel` object: Instead of session: the left frame {x, y, w, h} (accessibility points) that the Capsule's own session panel already holds. Only Chrome is moved, fitted from its right edge.
  - `ratio` number: The left window's share of the width, 0.2-0.5. Default 0.29.
  - `session` any: "front", "terminal", {"bundle": "com.apple.Terminal"} or {"pid": 123}.
  - `url` string: An http(s) page to open in the right window.
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `sideview.status`

Whether the side view is open, with its ratio and the two windows (app, title, frame). Local callers only.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

## sight

### `sight.frame`

One still of an agent's screen: a JPEG scaled to maxWidth (160-1280, default 480), base64. For a small "what the agent is doing now" view that refreshes on sight.stepped, never on a timer; the live view is sight.watch. Refused while a person is signing in on that computer (the shield). The Mac answers local_only.

- Input:
  - `target` string, required
  - `maxWidth` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `sight.now`

What is on one screen now: app, window, URL (without its query), the last step and who holds the keyboard. parts ["text"] adds the visible text and focused control, for mac only; parts ["controls"] adds an agent's controls (never their values). The Mac is never read for a caller from the tailnet.

- Input:
  - `target` string, required: "mac" or "agent:<name>"
  - `parts` list of "text" or "controls"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `sight.steps`

Recent steps on screens, newest first: action, a short summary, app, outcome, and the thread and tool call that made each. Filter by target or thread. The Mac's steps are left out for a caller from the tailnet.

- Input:
  - `limit` integer
  - `target` string
  - `thread` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `sight.targets`

Every screen Vyre can show: "mac" (this Mac, when the screen module runs here) and one "agent:<name>" per computer. Each row says kind, label, whether it is live, and who holds its keyboard.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `sight.watch`

Open an agent's screen: a one-use ticket for its Glass frame stream (computers.watch). surface names the person's screen, e.g. glass:<device>. The Mac is never streamed: it answers local_only.

- Input:
  - `target` string, required
  - `slow` boolean
  - `surface` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

## signin

### `signin.answer`

The owner's answer to the sign-in ask: { id, approve: true } with the presence proof signed over the card's payload_hash (Face ID on the phone), or { id, approve: false }. A no, a wrong proof or a timed-out ask changes nothing.

- Input:
  - `approve` boolean, required
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `signin.ask`

Ask the owner's phone to sign in this terminal. Answers { id, expires_in_s }; read the outcome with signin.status. Only from a terminal login the daemon can see; nothing is signed in until the owner approves. One ask at a time, open for 5 minutes.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `local`

### `signin.dev`

Development builds only: make a person session (a cookie) for the owner on the device node you name, marked as the stand-in's. Needs the dev stand-in file and a caller the daemon already counts as the owner.

- Input:
  - `node` string, required
  - `label` string
- Callers: `cli`, `local`

### `signin.end`

Sign this terminal out: its command-line session ends.

- Input: none
- Callers: `cli`, `local`

### `signin.pending`

The command-line sign-in waiting for the owner, as the phone shows it: { id, title, body, op, space, fields, payload_hash } to sign, or { none: true }.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `signin.status`

Where the sign-in ask stands: waiting, approved (with the credential, once, only to the terminal that asked), refused, or none.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: `cli`, `local`

## spaces

### `spaces.abilities`

What a person may do in a space right now (a temp's access ends on time). For other modules.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.admin-list`

The finished spaces a person owns or administers here: { spaces: [{ space, name, role }] }, and the identity's own name when it is this device's. For modules (pairing targets).

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.assess-computer`

What it means to keep a space on this computer: it is unreachable while it sleeps, is off or offline.

- Input:
  - `device` object
    - `alwaysOn` boolean
    - `id` string
    - `name` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.attest`

Sign a joiner's nonce with this home's key for a space it hosts: { pub, sig }. For the peer door (modules only).

- Input:
  - `nonce` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.brief`

The person's Spaces by name and role, and which one this home is: what an agent's environment brief says. Names and roles only. Modules only.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.cancel`

Stop creating a space and roll back what can be rolled back: the name, the key, a new server. Says what it could not remove.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `vpsToken` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.code.submit`

The new server sends the code its person typed (or, for a second server, with join). The two numbers must match, five tries, ten minutes.

- Input:
  - `code` string, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `join` string
  - `vpsToken` string
- Callers: `device`, `relay`, `tailnet`

### `spaces.compute.accept`

As a member: accept (or stop accepting) this space using your own computer for your own sessions. `terms` is the hash the space's terms show; if the space changed them you must look again.

- Input:
  - `enabled` boolean, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `terms` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.compute.allow`

As an owner or admin: allow (or stop allowing) this space's work to run on members' own computers. A member must still accept, and it covers only that member's own sessions on their own machine.

- Input:
  - `enabled` boolean, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `maxSessions` number
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.compute.may-run`

The scheduler asks: may this member's session run on this member's computer? Needs both grants and the same person on the session, the machine and the request.

- Input:
  - `machine` object, required
    - `owner` string, required
  - `session` object, required
    - `owner` string, required
    - `running` number
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.compute.status`

What the space and you have each said about running its work on your computer, and whether it is on.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.create`

Create a space and say where it will live: a server you have (the one command, then a code), a new server (DigitalOcean) or this computer. Runs step by step and can be resumed or cancelled.

- Input:
  - `home` object, required
    - `kind` one of "server", "vps", "this-computer", required
    - `confirmed` boolean
    - `device` object
    - `provider` string
    - `region` string
    - `size` string
    - `token` string
  - `name` string, required
  - `displayName` string
  - `storeChoice` "server" or "cancel"
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.devices.enrol`

Enrol a device of yours in a space: "Add to this device", one tap, nothing more asked. Leave device out for the device you are on.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `device` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.devices.enrolled`

Whether a device is enrolled in a space (true when the device has no list yet). For the kernel and other modules, which refuse a device that is not.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.devices.lend`

Lend one of your computers to a space, or stop. The first time for a device in a space needs your Face ID or fingerprint; stopping never does.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `on` boolean, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `member` string: Stopping only: the person whose computer it is, when an owner or admin of the space stops it from the space's own server (the computer is then named by the id the space gives it).
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.devices.lend.status`

Whether a device of yours is lent to a space, when it was first lent and who allowed it. Only the device's person and the space's owners and admins can ask.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.devices.list`

The same as spaces.devices.spaces, for the Device screen: the spaces a device of yours reaches, whether it is enrolled in each, and whether you lend it to that space.

- Input:
  - `device` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.devices.remove`

Remove one of your devices from one space. The space (and the kernel) refuse it from then on; your other spaces and the device's other access are untouched.

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.devices.restore`

Enrol a device of yours in a space again (the same as spaces.devices.enrol).

- Input:
  - `device` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.devices.set`

Set the whole list of spaces a device is enrolled in, as pairing does: every space you are in comes pre-ticked and the person unticks the ones to leave out. Leave device out for the device you are on.

- Input:
  - `spaces` list of string, required
  - `device` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.devices.spaces`

The spaces a device of yours can reach, each with your role there and whether this device is enrolled in it (`enrolled: false` is the space's "Add to this device"). Leave device out for the device you are on.

- Input:
  - `device` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.get`

One space: its name, home, owners and warnings.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.host-here`

On a server: host a new space in THIS home's kernel for its owner (called by the owner's device over the paired session when a space is made with this server as its home). Answers { space }. Idempotent when given the id. Needs no presence proof: the owner check is the gate.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `acceptBuiltinStore` boolean
  - `id` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.alias`

The DNS TXT record to publish at _vyre-id.<your domain> so your own domain can sit on top of your Vyre name (or a space's). Publish it, then call spaces.identity.alias.add.

- Input:
  - `domain` string, required
  - `name` string
  - `space` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.alias.add`

Add an own domain to your Vyre name (or a space's) once its TXT record is published.

- Input:
  - `domain` string, required
  - `name` string
  - `space` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.code.replace`

Make a new recovery code (and optionally a new recovery password); the old code stops working. The code comes back in this reply only.

- Input:
  - `password` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.contact.key`

As a recovery contact: make the approval key for someone's identity and give them its public half. This device keeps the private half and signs when they ask, after you approve.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.create`

Become yourself on this device: make its key and your identity, and finish the Vyre name that a reservation code (VYRE-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX, made at vyre.run/setup) holds. The recovery code comes back in this reply only: show it to the person once and never keep a copy. A recovery password is optional (four or more words is best); with one, the paper alone is not enough.

- Input:
  - `code` string
  - `deviceLabel` string
  - `name` string
  - `password` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.device`

This device's entry on its identity list: { deviceId, deviceKey }, or null when none is claimed. The public half only. For the daemon.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.devices`

The devices of a person you share a space with: each one's id and its key-agreement point, for wrapping a chat key. Public data only; a person you share no space with gives nothing.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.devices.read`

The devices of a person you share a space with: each one's id and its key-agreement point, for a first-party module that wraps a chat key. Public data only.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.enrol`

Put a newly paired device on this person's identity list. Signed by this device's entry; the device is a newcomer for 24 hours. For pairing.

- Input:
  - `publicKey` string, required
  - `agree` string
  - `attest` string
  - `enclave` string
  - `held` string
  - `label` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.entries`

Who can speak for you: your devices, your recovery code and your recovery contacts, with which are new sign-ins (under 24 hours old).

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.entry`

A device entry as the identity list holds it now, if it belongs to a member of the space: { eid, kind, pub }, else null. Read live every call. For the transport.

- Input:
  - `eid` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.entry.add`

Add a device (its public key from pairing) or a recovery contact (the key the contact made for you). Signed by this device. A sign-in under 24 hours old cannot add a contact.

- Input:
  - `publicKey` string, required
  - `kind` "device" or "contact"
  - `label` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.entry.remove`

Take a device or contact off your list in one tap. Any older device can remove a newcomer. A sign-in under 24 hours old can remove only newer sign-ins.

- Input:
  - `eid` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.evidence`

A person's identity chain and entries from the directory, verified, only if it is the given id's: { ops, entries }. Each entry says whether it is the founder and when it was added (signed time); the door decides what is young. For the home's invitee door.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `name` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.id`

This device's permanent identity id (and its own entry id on the list, `eid`), or null when none is claimed yet. The one place the id is kept is this module; pairing and install read it here, never keep their own. For modules.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.lookup`

A claimed Vyre name's identity list from the directory, verified, and only if it is the given id's: { entries }. Nothing is kept. For the pairing module.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `pin` object
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.name-of`

The claimed Vyre name for a person's id, verified: { name: 'alex.vyre.run' | null }. For modules.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `claimed` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.recover.approve`

As a recovery contact: approve someone's recovery request. Needs your approval on this device.

- Input:
  - `request` object, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.identity.recover.begin`

On a new device with nothing else: ask your recovery contacts. Returns a request to pass to two of them.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `deviceLabel` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.recover.code`

On a new device: take your identity back with the recovery code (and the recovery password if you set one). Works at once; the new sign-in is a newcomer for 24 hours.

- Input:
  - `code` string, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `deviceLabel` string
  - `password` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.recover.finish`

On the new device: put two contacts' approvals on the request and take your identity back.

- Input:
  - `approvals` list of object, required
    - `eid` string, required
    - `sig` string, required
  - `requestId` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.republish`

Put your identity's chain and each finished space's name in the directory again, for a directory that lost its claims (a test server that restarted). Your name is finished again with a fresh reservation code (`code`) when the directory no longer holds it. Says what it put back and what it could not.

- Input:
  - `code` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.resolve`

Look up a Vyre name (or an own domain) by its exact name and verify its list. Returns the kind, the permanent id and the list's head. There is no way to browse names.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `pin` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.self`

This device's identity id and name, or null when none is claimed. Read live every call. For other modules, so that nothing makes a second identity.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.sign`

Sign the transport's device proof (a message that starts with vyre-wink-peer-v2) with this device's key. Refuses anything else.

- Input:
  - `message` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.state`

A person's identity list as verified now: their entry ids and kinds. Read live each call. For the transport's personOf.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.identity.status`

This device's Vyre identity: its name, its permanent id and its place on the list. No key is ever shown.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.sync`

Check the directory for changes to your list: new sign-ins and removals (each is an alert), whether this device was removed, and whether the directory answered with a stale or different list.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.identity.unwrap-drop`

Open a file drop's wrapped key with this device's key-agreement key: the wrap and its associated data in, the unwrapped file key out. Only for a wrap whose associated data starts with vyre-drop-wrap. For first-party modules only.

- Input:
  - `aad` string, required
  - `wrap` object, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.invites.accept`

Join a space from its link, signing with this device's person key. When this device is the space's home the membership is made at once; otherwise the signed acceptance is returned for the home to redeem.

- Input:
  - `link` string, required
  - `pin` string
  - `presence_key` object
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.invites.confirm`

As the inviter of an admin or owner: confirm the fingerprint words the invitee reads to you. Their invite waits for this.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `words` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.invites.create`

Make a join link (https://<space>.vyre.run/join/...) for a role. A temp or member invite can name projects. Owners and admins only, unless the space lets managers invite.

- Input:
  - `role` one of "owner", "admin", "manager", "member", "temp", required
  - `space` string, required
  - `alias` string
  - `expires` number
  - `scope` list of string
  - `to` string
  - `ttlDays` number
  - `uses` number
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.invites.list`

Invites you made, or all you may manage as owner or admin. Never includes the link.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.invites.preview`

What a join link offers, before joining: the space, the role, what you will see and the button. Checks the link's signature against the space's pinned key. Shows nothing else.

- Input:
  - `link` string, required
  - `pin` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.invites.redeem`

The space's home checks a signed acceptance (from spaces.invites.accept on another device) and makes the membership. The link's signature, the person's own signature and the use count are the authority.

- Input:
  - `person` object, required
    - `id` string, required
    - `by` string
    - `name` string
    - `ops` list of object
    - `publicKey` string
  - `proof` string, required
  - `token` string, required
- Callers: `device`, `relay`, `tailnet`

### `spaces.invites.revoke`

Cancel an invite so its link stops working.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.label`

What a join card shows for a space the kernel hosts: its name and the four fingerprint words of its identity list and root key. With no space it answers for this home's own Space once it holds the root key. The kernel reads this on every invite card.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.list`

Spaces on this device that you created or belong to, with your role in each. For a space with a kernel the role is the kernel's answer. On a server that has no identity of its own (paired to yours), the spaces its kernel hosts for its owner.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.members.add`

Add a person (their per_ id or their Vyre name) with a role. A temp member needs scope and an end date. Making an owner needs the person's approval on their device.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `role` one of "owner", "admin", "manager", "member", "temp", required
  - `space` string, required
  - `expires` number
  - `scope` list of string
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.members.add-agent`

Add an agent (an assistant that does work in the space, for example as the doer of a task) to a space. The kernel's own actor membership: owners and admins only, under the person's proof.

- Input:
  - `agent` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.members.extend`

Give a temp member a later end date. This is a grant change, so it needs the person's approval on their device.

- Input:
  - `expires` number, required
  - `person` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.members.list`

Everyone in a space with their role, scope and end date, and any warnings (such as a single owner).

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.members.remove`

Remove a person from a space. A space always keeps at least one owner.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.members.set-role`

Change a person's role. Making someone an owner needs the person's approval on their device.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `role` one of "owner", "admin", "manager", "member", "temp", required
  - `space` string, required
  - `expires` number
  - `scope` list of string
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.members.transfer`

Hand a space to another member. The old owner becomes an admin (or the role you name). Needs the person's approval on their device.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `to` string, required
  - `demoteTo` one of "admin", "manager", "member"
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.membership`

A person's membership in a space, or null. For other modules to decide who may do what. For a space with a kernel it is the kernel's answer.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.merge-list`

The spaces a person is in, one entry each: { space, name, color, link }, for a device that merges spaces itself. For modules.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.move.plan`

The plan for moving a space to another home in one action: ordered steps, the checks each needs, nothing moves yet. Owners only.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `to` object, required
    - `kind` one of "server", "vps", "this-computer", required
    - `confirmed` boolean
    - `device` object
    - `provider` string
    - `region` string
    - `size` string
    - `token` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.moves.evidence`

In the SOURCE home: the signed evidence that you started a move of a project out of a space here, for the target to check. { evidence, pub, sig }; the Space's own key signs only what its own log says. `toName` and `pin` say which published space the move goes to: this home resolves that space's key now and holds the right to pull for that space only.

- Input:
  - `move_id` string, required
  - `pin` string, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `toName` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.moves.finish`

In the SOURCE home: the target's signed receipt arrived. Checked against the target space's published key (`toName`, `pin`), then the move is marked done here, and only then may anything be cleared from the source.

- Input:
  - `move_id` string, required
  - `pin` string, required
  - `receipt` object, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `toName` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.moves.pull`

In the SOURCE home: answer one request of a target home's pull (hello, auth, plan, records, file, sealed or done). For the daemon's peer door only: the target proves itself with its Space key, and nothing is served outside the plan this home recomputes under the mover. See lib/spaces/move-pull.js.

- Input:
  - `request` object, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.moves.pull-check-source`

In the TARGET home: is this signature the SOURCE space's, by its published key (`fromName`, `pin`)? The driver asks before it signs anything.

- Input:
  - `from` string, required
  - `fromName` string, required
  - `move_id` string, required
  - `nonce` string, required
  - `pin` string, required
  - `src_sig` string, required
  - `to` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.moves.pull-sign`

In the TARGET home: sign the pull proof for a move this space received, bound to both spaces, the move and the source's nonce, with this space's key. Only for a move this space has received (`project.move_in` in its log).

- Input:
  - `from` string, required
  - `move_id` string, required
  - `nonce` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.moves.receipt`

In the TARGET home, after the copy is checked: finish the move here and answer the receipt the SOURCE needs ({ body, pub, sig }), signed with this space's key over the counts and the root of the per-file hashes.

- Input:
  - `counts` object, required
  - `files_root` string, required
  - `move_id` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.moves.receive`

In the TARGET home: receive a project moved from a space on another home. `bundle` is the signed evidence; `fromName` and `pin` say which published space it names, the way an invite does.

- Input:
  - `bundle` object, required
  - `from` string, required
  - `fromName` string, required
  - `move_id` string, required
  - `pin` string, required
  - `plan_hash` string, required
  - `project` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.moves.source-channel`

In the TARGET home: where the SOURCE space's home can be reached, from its published directory record (`fromName`, `pin`): { relay, route, box }. The driver passes it to the home-to-home channel (`wink.home.call`). Refused unless the record names this very space id.

- Input:
  - `from` string, required
  - `fromName` string, required
  - `pin` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.owner.adopt`

For the pairing module, after it has PROVED the identity: make this home's owner (and its hosted Spaces') the person's identity id. Once only.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `name` string
  - `presence_key` object
    - `device` string, required
    - `key_id` string, required
    - `signer` string, required
    - `spki` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.owner.claimed`

For the pairing module: the identity id that took this home's owner place ({ claimed }), or { claimed: null } while the owner is still the first-start id. Read only.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.people`

The people of a space that hold an identity this device knows: its members and the person of a pending invite. For the transport's personOf.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.person.learn`

For the peer door: remember a person's Vyre name once the directory says it is theirs, so this home can find their identity list (member-device enrolment). Answers { known }.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.personal-host.set`

Choose which Cloud space keeps your encrypted personal items (Settings). It must be one you are in.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.presence.begin`

On a new device that has lost every presence key: ask the sealing process for the one-time token the recovery needs, for the key this device just made (its id and public key).

- Input:
  - `key_id` string, required
  - `spki` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.presence.recover`

After taking your identity back (the recovery code, or two contacts): give the sealing process the new presence key, vouched for by this device's own key on your list. The process checks your list; the new key counts as a newcomer for 24 hours.

- Input:
  - `key_id` string, required
  - `signer` string, required
  - `spki` string, required
  - `token` string, required
  - `attestation` object
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.presence.sync`

Send the sealing process your current identity list, so a device you removed loses its presence key at once.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.resume`

Continue creating a space from the last good step. Give a new name if the first was taken, confirm 'this computer' if asked, or paste the server token again.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `confirmThisComputer` boolean
  - `name` string
  - `vpsToken` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.retire-here`

On a server: take back a space that was only started here (a failed or cancelled create). Refuses a space with content (fail closed).

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
- Callers: any caller
- Needs a person present.

### `spaces.roles.names`

Read the display names of the five roles, or rename one (owner or admin). The ids never change.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `name` string
  - `role` one of "owner", "admin", "manager", "member", "temp"
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.self`

This device's person and the space a call is for (the one named, or the only one this person is in), for a module that has already taken the person from the call's kernel chain (ctx.kernel.chain(meta)): `person` is that chain's person, and it is this device's own only when it is the home's own person; anyone else is nobody. For modules.

- Input:
  - `person` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.server-of`

The paired server's device id that hosts a space this device made, or null. For modules.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.server.install`

The one command to run on a server, the prompt it will show, and the code to type there. For a space still being created it shows the current code; for a finished space it starts adding a server (as compute, or as the new home with moveHome).

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `moveHome` boolean
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.server.of`

The paired server that hosts this space: { device }, or null when this computer does not know one. For the Wink module.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.servers`

The servers this device is paired to, for "Set up My Cloud": { servers: [{ id, name }] }, where `id` is what `spaces.create` takes as `home.device.id`. Empty when none is paired.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.setup.claim`

Continue setting up a space on this device: setup moves here from the device it was on, and the state comes back. Only the person who is setting it up can do this.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.setup.save`

Keep where setup has got to for a space you are setting up (one of look, members, ai, connectors, kit), so another device can carry on. Send setup: null when the last step is done. Only the device setup is on may save; no secret, code or key is kept.

- Input:
  - `setup` object or null, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.status`

Where creating a space has got to: each step, what it is waiting for, and why anything failed, in plain words.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.storage.delete`

Delete one of your objects. With `expected` (its sha256, or null for "must not exist") it deletes only if it is still what you last saw, else answers { ok: false, sha256 } and deletes nothing. Never refused for the cap.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `expected` string or null
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.storage.get`

Read one of your objects: { data (base64), sha256 }, or null when it is not there.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.storage.list`

Your objects under a prefix, one level: `names`, and `entries` ({ name, sha, size }) to compare against.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `prefix` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.storage.put`

Keep an object (ciphertext, base64) in your own storage on a space this server hosts. Refused once your storage reaches the cap the owner set.

- Input:
  - `data` string, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.storage.put-if`

Keep an object only if it is still what you last saw: `expected` is its sha256 in hex, or null when it should not exist yet. Answers { ok, sha256 }, with the sha256 that is there now.

- Input:
  - `data` string, required
  - `expected` string or null, required
  - `name` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.storage.set-cap`

As an owner: set the storage cap in bytes (0 for none) for one member, or for everyone with person "*".

- Input:
  - `bytes` number, required
  - `person` string, required
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.storage.usage`

How much of your storage on this space you have used, and the cap.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.sweep`

End temp memberships and invites whose time has come, and say so (member.expired, invite.expired). Runs by itself an hour apart.

- Input: none
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.tier`

Which tier a space is on (basic or cloud), and the Cloud spaces this person is in. For a module that must refuse on a Basic personal space (Planner, tasks). With no space named, the home's own.

- Input:
  - `space` string
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spaces.time-zone.set`

As an owner or admin: set a space's home time zone (an IANA zone such as America/Los_Angeles). Tasks, Flow schedules and business hours read it.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
  - `zone` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.upgrade.plan`

What moving your Personal space to My Cloud would carry: records by type, what cannot be carried, and the hash your one approval is bound to. Reads only.

- Input:
  - `to` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.upgrade.receipt`

In MY CLOUD's home: say what this space holds of the objects an upgrade carried, signed with this space's key. It reads its OWN records under your chain and answers { body, pub, sig }: the count and the root of the per-object hashes. The Personal space freezes only on this.

- Input:
  - `from` string, required
  - `objects` list, required
  - `space` string, required
  - `upgrade_id` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.upgrade.run`

Move your Personal space to My Cloud with one approval: `plan_hash` is the plan you were shown. Answers what moved and, by name, anything that did not. My Cloud's name, the pinned version of its list and the paired server are worked out from the space this device made (override with `toName`, `pin`, `server`). It asks My Cloud for its signed receipt: only then does this space point to My Cloud and stop taking new records.

- Input:
  - `plan_hash` string, required
  - `to` string, required
  - `approve_proof` object
  - `pin` string
  - `server` string
  - `toName` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spaces.upgrade.wrap-key`

In MY CLOUD's home: answer this space's sealed-value wrapping key, signed with this space's own key, so the device can check it came from the Space it names before it approves carrying sealed values there.

- Input:
  - `space` string, required
- Callers: any caller

## spend

### `spend.check`

Whether a provider may spend now: { ok, capped, spent, cap, left, line? }. Ask before a call that costs; at the cap answer from what is already known and show the line. No cap set means ok.

- Input:
  - `provider` string
- Callers: any caller

### `spend.raise`

Raise a provider's daily cap (provider: all is the cap over every provider together): to (new cap in USD) or by (add this much), or off: true for no cap. The person's own surfaces only. Takes effect at once; a paused thread goes on when it is resumed, and the cap says again tomorrow if reached.

- Input:
  - `by` number
  - `off` boolean
  - `provider` string
  - `to` number
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `spend.record`

Put one spend in the ledger: { provider, account?, purpose, agent?, thread?, usd, tokens_in?, tokens_out?, calls?, estimated? }. Returns { capped, spent, cap }. At the provider's cap it says so once a day (spend.capped) and pauses the thread it names. For Vyre's own modules.

- Input:
  - `purpose` string, required
  - `account` string
  - `agent` string
  - `calls` integer
  - `estimated` boolean
  - `provider` string
  - `thread` string
  - `tokens_in` integer
  - `tokens_out` integer
  - `usd` number
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `spend.summary`

Today's spend (UTC) per provider with its cap, and the rows behind it: { day, all: { spent, cap, left, capped }, providers: [{ provider, spent, cap, left, capped, calls, estimated }], rows }; all is every provider together against spend.all.daily_usd. day is YYYY-MM-DD for an earlier one.

- Input:
  - `day` string
- Callers: any caller

## statusline

### `statusline.line`

The Vyre status line as it is right now, recomputed: what needs the user, the box, the assistant.

- Input: none
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

## stream

### `stream.catchup`

What happened in a chat since you last read it: the last messages (who said what), the steps taken, questions still open, who joined or left. Plain facts from the chat's own log; nothing is summarised by a model.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `as` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.export-chat`

A chat's logged frames and member rows, for the chat upgrade. First-party modules only.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `stream.import-chat`

Put a chat's frames and member rows back (the other end of the chat upgrade); with fresh: true, a chat that already has frames here is left as it is. First-party modules only.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `frames` list, required
  - `members` list, required
  - `fresh` boolean
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `stream.keep`

Keep one answer of a fan-out set; the others stay, quieter.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `group` string, required
  - `keep` string, required
  - `as` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.mark-read`

Move the caller's read marker in a session forward to a cursor. The caller's other open connections hear it; nobody else does.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `upto` integer, required
  - `as` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.open`

A one-use ticket (15 s) for a chat's stream at path, resuming after cursor from (0 for everything the log holds). Also the log's head and floor: a from below floor will be sent a reset.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `as` string
  - `from` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.open-peer`

Open the session's stream over the Wink peer wire (for a paired device): answers { stream, session, viewer, head, floor }; the frames then arrive as peer stream messages for `stream`, resuming after cursor from. Only over the peer wire.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `as` string
  - `from` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.pin`

Pin a message in a group chat (on: false unpins it).

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `message` string, required
  - `as` string
  - `on` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.react`

React to a message in a group chat with an emoji (on: false takes it back).

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `emoji` string, required
  - `message` string, required
  - `as` string
  - `on` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.second-opinion`

Ask another assistant or model of this chat for its own answer to the same question, on an answer you are reading: { chat, message, to }. The chat shows one short line from you; the other assistant is also given the question, the answer and the last turns from the chat itself. A model the chat does not have yet (codex, grok, provider/model) joins in the same step.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `message` string, required
  - `to` string, required
  - `as` string
  - `surface` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.send`

Say something in a group chat (a stream session with several people and assistants). The words are the caller's, appended first; then routing decides who answers (an @mention, the default assistant when no person is talking to a person, or the assistants named in to) and each gets the words in its own thread; its replies appear in the group with that assistant as author and the caller as acts_for. Two or more answering assistants make a fan-out set. People and assistants join by being named in people and assistants (an assistant needs a cwd to work in). Retry with the same message id and nothing is said twice. A private message is sent with enc { alg, kid, ct } and no text: an opaque ciphertext made on the person's device, stored and relayed as it is, never parsed, routed to no assistant and kept out of search, memory and export.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `as` string
  - `assistants` list of any
  - `cwd` string
  - `default` string
  - `enc` object
    - `alg` string, required
    - `ct` string, required
    - `kid` string, required
  - `group` string
  - `mentions` list of string
  - `message` string
  - `mode` "steer" or "queue"
  - `name` string
  - `people` list of any
  - `reply_to` string
  - `surface` string
  - `text` string
  - `to` list of string
  - `tz` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `stream.typing`

Tell the chat you are typing (on: false: you stopped). Others see "typing" for a few seconds; nothing is kept.

- Input:
  - `chat` string, required
  - `as` string
  - `on` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

## suggest

### `suggest.offer`

A module adds a suggestion source: tool, one of its own tools (<module>.<name>), and the kinds it answers. The tool gets { prefix, text, surface, context, limit } and has 25 ms; it returns items [{ label, kind?, insert?, detail?, action?, id?, score? (0 to 1), last? }]. Offer again at every start; it replaces the last.

- Input:
  - `kinds` list of one of "mention", "command", "account", "entity", "phrase", "file", "time", required
  - `tool` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `suggest.picked`

The user chose a suggestion: its kind, source and id as suggest.query gave them. It ranks higher next time; the lift fades over weeks.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `kind` one of "mention", "command", "account", "entity", "phrase", "file", "time", required
  - `source` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `suggest.query`

What the user may be typing, at the cursor: names after @ (agents, projects, threads, people), commands after /, and otherwise entities, phrases, accounts and upcoming times for the last word. Answers from cached lists in a few ms; a module's offered source that misses 25 ms is named in late. Returns { items: [{ kind, sub?, label, insert, detail?, action?, source, id, score }], ms, late? }; insert replaces the token at the cursor.

- Input:
  - `surface` string, required
  - `text` string, required
  - `context` object
  - `cursor` integer
  - `limit` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

## sync

### `sync.consent`

Turn a paired peer's session import on or off, on the box's own record: never the device's say-so. Off only stops new uploads: nothing already sent is touched. sync.delete removes what a device sent, as its own action. planHash and included, when the surface reviewed a sync.scan plan with the person, are stored with the consent: sync.delete.import can later remove just that import by its planHash, and every project folder not in included is refused by sync.upload.plan and sync.upload.start, not merely left untagged. The picker's exclusions are enforced, not advisory. folders (Vyre Drive step 4) is the person's confirmed folder-to-project mapping, sync.scan's own proposed slug or whatever they typed instead: the first file that lands for a mapped folder creates that project (if the slug is new) and attaches the folder with projects.add-workspace. A folder left out of folders is synced but attached to no project.

- Input:
  - `machine` string, required
  - `on` boolean, required
  - `folders` list of object: The confirmed folder-to-project mapping: name is one of sync.scan's own folder names, project the id to attach it to.
    - `name` string, required
    - `project` string, required
  - `included` list of string: Project folder names (sync.scan's own names) this plan lets in. Omitted or on: false: no restriction.
  - `planHash` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Registered only on the box.

### `sync.delete`

Delete everything a device sent and everything derived from it: synced/<machine>/ and its quarantine, then sync.deleted. Its own person-only action, never implied by unpairing or turning sync off. Without confirm: true, answers a preview (file and byte counts) and deletes nothing; the person sees the counts, then calls it again with confirm: true.

- Input:
  - `machine` string, required
  - `confirm` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Registered only on the box.

### `sync.delete.import`

Delete just one approved import's files (those sync.consent's planHash tagged as they landed), leaving anything a separately-approved plan sent for the same device untouched. Its own person-only action, same as sync.delete. Without confirm: true, answers a preview (file and byte counts) and deletes nothing.

- Input:
  - `machine` string, required
  - `planHash` string, required
  - `confirm` boolean
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Registered only on the box.

### `sync.scan`

What this device would offer to sync to the box (Vyre Drive's what-to-sync picker): every project folder under this device's own Claude Code folder (~/.claude/projects or CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/projects), each with its session-file count and total size, so the person sees what is there and can leave folders out before turning sync.consent on. Read-only: nothing is sent, nothing is opened, only sizes are read. Each folder also carries `project`, a proposed project id (lib/project-id.js's slugify of the folder name) for Vyre Drive step 4's folder-to-project mapping: a suggestion only, the person confirms or edits it at sync.consent time; nothing here creates a project or attaches anything. planHash stands for the choice made here: pass it straight to sync.consent's own planHash, so an approved import is tied to what was actually reviewed, not a plan that silently drifted.

- Input:
  - `exclude` list of string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`
- Registered only on the Mac (local).

### `sync.send`

Send this device's own files to the box: sync.upload.plan/start/chunk/finish per file, a per-file ack, and a completion summary (sync.sent, sent/failed/quarantined). mode: "once" sends this list and stops; "sync" is the same send, and the idle-batched watch for new and changed files after it is not yet built (see team/archive/work-journals/federation.md). Only a file inside this device's own Claude Code folder (~/.claude or CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR), no symlink escape, under the size cap, is ever read.

- Input:
  - `files` list of object, required
    - `bytes` number, required
    - `hash` string, required
    - `path` string, required
    - `rel` string, required
  - `mode` "once" or "sync", required
- Callers: `module`
- Registered only on the Mac (local).

### `sync.upload.cancel`

Give up on an open upload before it finishes: drops its temp file and its slot, freeing one of the peer's 8 open uploads without waiting for the idle sweep. Not an error if the id is already gone (finished, expired, or never existed); cancel always succeeds.

- Input:
  - `upload` string, required
  - `companion` string
- Callers: `agent`, `device`, `space`, `tailnet`
- Registered only on the box.

### `sync.upload.chunk`

One chunk of an upload's bytes, at an exact offset. Internal: the daemon's own route calls this after reading the request body.

- Input:
  - `data` any, required
  - `offset` number, required
  - `upload` string, required
  - `companion` string
- Callers: `agent`, `device`, `space`, `tailnet`
- Registered only on the box.

### `sync.upload.finish`

Verify and land a finished upload: checks its hash, scrubs it for secrets, and renames it into synced/<machine>/ (or quarantines it).

- Input:
  - `hash` string, required
  - `upload` string, required
  - `companion` string
- Callers: `agent`, `device`, `space`, `tailnet`
- Registered only on the box.

### `sync.upload.plan`

For a paired peer's own connection: which of its files are new, changed, already here, or outside the approved plan's included folders (excluded, sync.upload.start refuses these too, not merely reported), and its quota. Internal to the device's sender.

- Input:
  - `files` list of object, required
    - `bytes` number, required
    - `hash` string, required
    - `path` string, required
  - `companion` string
- Callers: `agent`, `device`, `space`, `tailnet`
- Registered only on the box.

### `sync.upload.start`

Start (or resume) sending one file: offset is 0 for new, or how many bytes the box already holds for a retry of the exact same path and hash.

- Input:
  - `bytes` number, required
  - `hash` string, required
  - `path` string, required
  - `companion` string
- Callers: `agent`, `device`, `space`, `tailnet`
- Registered only on the box.

## system

### `system.build`

The release's signed record of the web app this daemon serves at /app/: appbuild.json (the sha256 of every file of the build), the signed SHA256SUMS that lists it, and SHA256SUMS.sig. A client (the Mac window) verifies the signature with the release key and the list's own hash, then checks every file it is served. A development build has none.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `system.echo`

Returns what it was given. For checking that tools and the rules path work.

- Input:
  - `text` string, required
- Callers: any caller

### `system.info`

What this machine is running: Vyre version and the commit it was built from, role, host and platform, the owner's name as onboarding saved it and their fingerprint8 (a short, stable, non-secret fingerprint of owner.id, base64url, for a surface's avatar), the assistant's name (which every surface uses to label replies; null: surfaces say "Vyre") and its own fingerprint8 (same formula, kind "assistant", also base64url), and network.origins: the other sites (Vyre's hosted app) that may call this box from the owner's browser ([] when off).

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `system.modules`

The modules on this machine and the slots each shows in the app: { modules: [{ name, version, state, now: [tool, ...] }] }. A module's Now card is a tool named in shows.deck as now:<tool>; the app calls it and draws what it answers.

- Input: none
- Callers: any caller

### `system.rename`

Rename this server: its display name, a label the person chooses (not its vyre.run address). It is shown wherever this machine appears, and a phone sees it when pairing. An empty name goes back to the default.

- Input:
  - `name` string, required
- Callers: any caller

## tasks

### `tasks.decide`

Approve or reject a task waiting for a check. The person's own act, with their presence.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `outcome` "approved" or "rejected", required
  - `reason` string
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `tasks.get`

One task, or null when it is not there or not the caller's to read.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `tasks.list`

Tasks, oldest first: by record, doer, checker or state. Only what the caller may read.

- Input:
  - `checker` string
  - `doer` string
  - `record` string
  - `space` string
  - `state` list of one of "waiting", "ready", "working", "needs_check", "stuck", "done", "skipped"
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `tasks.move`

Move a task: start it, flag it stuck (with a reason), skip it, or fix a stuck one. Which kernel act that is, and whether the caller may, is the kernel's.

- Input:
  - `id` string, required
  - `to` one of "working", "stuck", "skipped", "ready", required
  - `reason` string
  - `space` string
  - `suggested_fix` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `tasks.request`

Ask for a task: a title, its output, a doer and, when it needs one, a checker. The kernel writes its id, state and who asked.

- Input:
  - `task` object, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

### `tasks.submit`

The doer hands in what it made. The kernel checks the output and moves the task on (to needs_check or done).

- Input:
  - `evidence` object, required
  - `id` string, required
  - `space` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mobile`

## team

### `team.act.target`

Registry only: the destination an asked team call must be said for. Answers { to: [key] }.

- Input:
  - `input` object, required
  - `tool` string, required
- Callers: other modules only (internal: `vyre call` answers no_such_tool)

### `team.add`

Add a teammate to a project: a role (how sessions address it, e.g. "design"), a brief (what work goes to it) and, optionally, instructions, tools and isolation. Makes agent <role>-<project>. isolation: "worktree" gives it its own git worktree and branch, and brings an "integrator" teammate along the first time, which merges finished work into the project's own branch; when the project's home is not a git repo it falls back to isolation: "folder" instead (shared with any other folder-isolated teammate), saying so in the answer's `notice`.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
  - `role` string, required
  - `brief` string
  - `helper_model` string
  - `instructions` string
  - `isolation` one of "worktree", "folder", "none"
  - `model` string
  - `tools` list of string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `team.ask`

Send work to a project's teammate by role ("design", "backend", ...): {to, text, refs?, priority?, wait?, project?, model?}. model (a provider such as codex or grok, provider/model, or a Claude model name) runs this request in a session of its own on that model, through the same Gate and spend as any teammate session. Queues a request in the teammate's serial inbox and returns {request, state, position}. wait (at most 30s) returns the result if it finishes by then. The result otherwise comes back later as a message in the calling thread.

- Input:
  - `text` string, required
  - `to` string, required
  - `key` string
  - `model` string
  - `priority` one of "urgent", "normal", "low"
  - `project` string
  - `refs` list of string
  - `wait` boolean
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `team.cancel`

Cancel a queued request. A running request is interrupted only by a person (stop its teammate's session, or team.fail from inside it).

- Input:
  - `request` string, required
- Callers: `agent`, `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `device`, `local`, `mcp`, `mobile`, `module`, `space`, `tailnet`

### `team.charter.accept`

Accept (or, with decline: true, drop) the charter a session drafted for a teammate: it becomes a new version, written as the person's own act. The person's surfaces only.

- Input:
  - `decline` boolean
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `team.charter.diff`

What a charter version changed: the version's text beside the one before it (null for the first), who wrote it and how. What the Deck's "charter changed by <agent>" card shows before a one-tap team.charter.revert.

- Input:
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
  - `version` integer
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `team.charter.draft`

Write (or rewrite) a teammate's charter from what the project already knows: its brief, its role, the project's context and the teammate's notes, plus anything in from (a line or a conversation summary). Saved as a new version, and returned so the person can read and edit it. Nobody has to hand-write what a teammate is.

- Input:
  - `from` string
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`, `module`

### `team.charter.get`

A teammate's charter: what it is for and how it works, the current version's text, who wrote it and when, or null when it has none yet. A teammate may read its own.

- Input:
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `team.charter.history`

Every version of a teammate's charter, newest first.

- Input:
  - `limit` integer
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `mcp`

### `team.charter.revert`

Make an older charter version the current one again, as a new version so the revert can be undone too.

- Input:
  - `version` integer, required
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `team.charter.set`

Write a teammate's charter (a new version; the old ones stay). It adds to the teammate's system prompt and never replaces Vyre's own rules; a live thread starts fresh at its next request so the new charter applies. At most 8000 characters. Person-only: an agent or a session proposes the text to the person, who writes it.

- Input:
  - `text` string, required
  - `note` string
  - `project` string
  - `role` string
  - `teammate` string
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `team.default.get`

Whether new-work steers to teammates by default in this project (docs/design/teammates.md section 1): the append line and @role's create-on-first-use. On unless a person has turned it off. {project} -> {project, enabled}.

- Input:
  - `project` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`, `module`

### `team.default.set`

Turn the default-to-teammates policy on or off for a project: no append line and no @role create-on-first-use while off. Existing teammates keep working either way; this is about steering new work, not removing what is already there. {project, enabled}.

- Input:
  - `enabled` boolean, required
  - `project` string, required
- Callers: `capsule`, `cli`, `deck`, `local`

### `team.done`

The teammate itself closes its running request with a result. request may be left out; it defaults to the teammate's one running request. Refused if the notes have not changed since the request started, unless notes: "unchanged" is given with a reason (a request that genuinely needed none). Never callable for another teammate's request.

- Input:
  - `result` string, required
  - `notes` "unchanged"
  - `reason` string
  - `request` string
  - `result_refs` list of string
